# OLYMPUS: design

The hub of the design. It holds the vision, the pillars, the decisions every system obeys, and the index of the detailed sections in `docs/design/`. When a section and this file disagree, this file wins until one of them is corrected; correct it in the same change.

Started 2026-11-05 from the user's brief (kickoff, quoted below) and from the research notes in `docs/research/` (twelve deep reads of `../longhaul` and `../tradecraft`, with screenshots in `docs/research/shots/`, kept on this Mac only and not committed: 27 MB of the other games' screens).

## The brief (the user, 2026-11-05)

Verbatim, the user's spelling kept. Sections cite the brief here, not through the research notes.

> Trying out ultracode. New project, a game, I used to be partial to browser builds because portable and easy to spin up but I will let you decide the whole tech stack and ask questions about specs you can't just guess. Tell me whether you want to install some software for optimal results. The goal here is to run as long as necessary and use as much tokens as necessary to achieve an excellent result. First of all the main idea for this project : This will be a video-game. Context : Hard scifi expanse based game where we will play Martian military in as realistic way as possible. Please read the ../longhaul project docs to understand the setting we already built, but only refactor the tech from there if it's the best way. The specifics now and compared to this game is that we will play not as a bodyless ship that somehow manages the crew, but we will play as a career military person who can be (or become) a foot soldier (with gameplay like getting on a drop ship to be deployed for peace keeping on Europa, then pretty realistic gameplay where we have to manage the day to day of that life (slow paced game, with emphasis on realism and depth like managing hygiene levels, weapon controls like managing safety, muzzle discipline, fitness, medical aid and healing etc.). But the player can also become part of special operations teams and while still having to manage their own bodily needs and their weapon, will be dropped in much different special forces settings. The main things I want about this game is 1) Realism in physics, air, gravity, space being big and travel taking a long time, health effects under G and not under G, 2) Good gameplay, 3) High Resolution Pixel Art for close up gameplay, 4) The game will be mostly top down at different scales (look at longhaul for telescope views of space objects like planets and stars and how ship tactical maps are drawn it will be something like that I'm looking for when for instance looking at a tactical screen on a ship). Please build the game from the ground UP, meaning give me functional test versions that we can update with a promotion script to test the latest stable version you built. The thing is that you will be autonomously building the game to a final version that given the amount of tokens will take several days to really finish since I want a full 1.0 game fully featured and debugged in the end. So tell me in chat when you drop new versions, keep the documentation updated, make sure to keep your session alive with long running caffeinate commands and make sure the game is fun. The itch I'm trying to scratch with this game is the itch of space world (air management, gravity effects, water management, single player survival with bodily functions, realism and complexity where it makes sense with for instance guns being played with details like reloading being a remove magazine, put other magazine when we have magazines, clear chamber, aim the gun etc. Things must take time and not feel rushed with the ability to do activities that skip time. We won't be doing things like scrubbing our legs in the shower, but we will have to manage body hygiene with world appliances like a shower, and taking a shower will take a chunk of time forward on the clock. The main moving around and UI should look somehting like (you don't have to use the same tech, this is just artistic direction and controls) in ../tradecraft, with ship interiors, planetary surfaces, habitats etc. Features should include, ship combat, ship boarding, combat on planetary grounds etc. There should always be a base where we go back to do things like eat and shower at the end of the day etc. Game should perform as well as possible and as many parts of the background generation need to be done in such a way that we don't have to render actual graphics but the game should feel immersive. Examples : You start as a marine on Mars, out of basic training and train on Mars on standby for a week or two, with the everyday training looking like what it should be, full days (Mars days not 24h) moving in real time until we do things that spend time like eating (some degree of splash screen to illustrate the action and a time fast forward). Shooting should play as in Tradecraft (../tradecraft), with weapon handling. The character gameplay should also be inspired by tradecraft with skills and RPG like resolution of challenges (like whether a shot hits or not). NPCs should also be well built and reactive, smart, have their own lives. Some NPCs will be fully fledged and permanent, and crowds will be more surface level and serve the purpose of filling the world. There is an inordinate amount of work just to spec this out fully, you will have a lot of time. Ask any question you need to ask along the way and do release stable versions as you keep on working.

Decided with the user at kickoff (2026-11-05):
- **Clock:** 1:1 real time while you act. Activities skip time.
- **Death:** chosen per career at new game. **Ironman** keeps one autosave (plus a hidden recovery copy, offered only when the save is damaged: TECH §7.2), and death ends the career. **Standard** lets you save anywhere and load after death. JSON export and import exist either way.
- **Versions:** Claude cuts patches and bumps the minor at each tested milestone. 1.0 is the user's call. The play copy moves only when the user promotes it.
- **GitHub:** public repo `mramad3us/olympus`. Every release is pushed with its tag.

## Pitch

You are a Martian marine. Out of boot camp, you are posted to a rifle platoon at a Marine base in the Mariner Valley. There you live the sol: reveille in the squad bay, the head, chow, PT and drills at one g in the Wheel, a spinning hall under the base that keeps your body ready for Earth gravity, the range under 0.38 g, suit drills, classes, weapon cleaning, and lights out. Then orders come. You ride a shuttle to Phobos, then a troopship to Jupiter under thrust for days, and the flip, and a drop. Then you keep the peace in an ice miners' town under Europa's shell, where a day outside is a lethal dose of radiation and the air inside is rationed. Do well and you can try out for the reconnaissance companies. Those take you to places no line marine sees: a station boarded in vacuum, a derelict, a dome on Ganymede.

The game is your body and your rifle, played in real time and in detail, with the clock jumping forward whenever something simply takes time. Above you, a solar system runs as LONGHAUL's does. The powers move toward war and back, fleets move on real orbits, and news comes at the speed of light. Your career is pushed around by all of it.

**The name.** OLYMPUS is named for Olympus Mons, Mars' great volcano (the user, 2026-11-06: "Olympus the name of the game is inspired by Olympus Mons on Mars"). Every map of Mars in the game shows it, named; its role in play is found as the game grows (the decision log, 2026-11-06).

## Pillars

1. **The body is the game.** Air, water, food, sleep, hygiene, bladder and bowel, heat, gravity, G-load, radiation and wounds are each a real, numbered model with real consequences. None of them is a bar to keep green for its own sake. Each one changes what you can do and how well.
2. **The gun is a machine.** Every weapon is a stateful object: safety, magazine, chamber, condition, what you know about it. Every action on it takes time and a skill roll. Muzzle discipline matters everywhere, not just at the range.
3. **Skill and dice decide execution; the player decides what to try.** This is tradecraft's resolution, used across the whole game: d20 + skill + named modifiers against a DC, and every roll can be read in the log. The player never aims with reflexes. Training and the career run the same mechanics, nothing watered down for either; experience shows as smoothness and speed (T's automation, the drills' bundles, faster and surer steps), never as steps removed or rules relaxed (the user, 2026-11-07: "make sure actual gameplay is consistent with training, no watering down but factor in experience in how smooth and fast we do things").
4. **Time is a currency.** At 1:1 the world is alive and you walk through it. Every action that takes time jumps the clock, behind an illustrated splash. There is no bare waiting and no fast travel that skips play. Long voyages and quiet stretches are lived as chapters with choices.
5. **Space is big and physics is honest.** Real orbits, real transit times, felt G, light lag, vacuum and pressure. Everything the screen shows is what the sim says, at true scale and in true time.
6. **People with lives.** Your squad, your NCOs and the people on base are persistent, with schedules, needs, moods, memories, relationships and careers of their own. Crowds are cheap and fill the world. Nobody pops in or out in view.
7. **Mistakes cost.** The game is lethal and the consequences are real (tradecraft: "no karma, only consequences"). Discipline is real as well: a negligent discharge, a missed formation or a dirty rifle all have in-world results.
8. **Fun first among the real.** Realism is in service of play. Complexity goes where it makes better decisions or better stories. Wherever it would only be chores, an activity skips it with an outcome. Each sol needs a rhythm (calm routine, then a spike, then the base at night), and each week needs a story beat.

## The loop at five scales

| Scale | What you do | Clock |
|---|---|---|
| Seconds | walk, look, talk, handle the weapon, fight (stop motion), give aid | 1:1, 20 Hz; stop motion in shooting and CQC; time stands still while you talk |
| Hours | the Plan of the Day: formations, training blocks, duties, meals, free time. Live a block or skip it with an outcome | 1:1 between blocks; skips with splash |
| Days to weeks | a voyage, a later dwell or standby (never the first standby); a deployment rotation | chapters for the voyage and the later dwell: key days played in full, quiet days chosen and summarised; a deployment is lived by default, with chapter days opt-in from its third week (CAREER §2.2) |
| Months | deployments, schools, selection, leave | chapters with papers |
| A career | rank, qualifications, scars, friends, the memorial | the world's own timeline |

A typical **active sol plays in 40 to 70 real minutes**. That covers two to four set pieces lived in full (the range, a suit drill, a patrol, a fight in the rec room), the walking and talking between them, and skips for meals, the shower, classes and sleep. Tradecraft targeted 20–40 minutes; OLYMPUS is slower by request ("slow paced"). **The first standby is lived sol by sol at 1:1**: only activities skip time (the brief: "full days (Mars days not 24h) moving in real time until we do things that spend time like eating"). From 0.2, a truly quiet sol in a later dwell week or on holiday routine can be spent down to a minute or two of choices ("Call it a day" at your rack, TIME §9.7) (my call).

## Settled decisions (every section obeys these)

### Setting
- **The world is LONGHAUL's.** It uses the same solar system, ephemeris, powers and sub-factions (UN, MCR, OPA, independents, pirate clans; inside the MCR: the MCRN Admiralty, Martian Naval Intelligence (MNI), the Congressional Unity Caucus, Hecate Arms & Drive, and Martian Security), politics model and ship classes. Its names and voice carry over ("Plant Trees Anyway", "Mind the Seals").
- **Mars is terraformed, a little** (the user, 2026-10-25, LONGHAUL v1.6.0). That means 52 kPa at datum, a 19 km scale height, a young northern sea, and lowlands you can breathe in.
  - **One correction for honest air:** LONGHAUL's 8% CO₂ would be hypercapnic, so OLYMPUS uses **N₂ 65.4 / O₂ 32 / Ar 2 / CO₂ 0.6 %**. The O₂ partial pressure then follows altitude:

    | Place | Altitude | Total pressure | O₂ | Inspired O₂ (PiO₂) | Feels like on Earth (by PiO₂) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Mariner Valley floor | −5 km | 67.7 kPa | 21.6 kPa | 19.7 kPa | sea level |
    | Base Melas | −4.5 km | 65.9 kPa | 21.1 kPa | 19.1 kPa | sea level |
    | Datum | 0 km | 52 kPa | 16.6 kPa | 14.6 kPa | about 2,400 m |
    | Highlands | +5 km | 40 kPa | 12.8 kPa | 10.8 kPa | about 4,300 m: hard work, mountain sickness within hours |
    | Tharsis domes | +10 km | 30.7 kPa | 9.8 kPa | 7.8 kPa | about 6,700 m: about 8 min of useful consciousness for the unacclimatised; masks or domes |
    | Olympus Mons summit | +21.9 km | 16.4 kPa | 5.3 kPa | 3.2 kPa | above 11,000 m: unconscious in under a minute without a mask |

    PiO₂ = O₂ fraction × (P − 6.27 kPa), the water vapour at body temperature taken off (BODY §11.1 owns the physiology; SETTING §4.2 the full table).
  - The CO₂ is about ten times Earth's by partial pressure in the valley (0.40 kPa at Melas against 0.042 kPa), seven times at datum: the air is "stuffy", and newcomers get headaches.
  - **What keeps it warm** (my call): 0.6% CO₂ is less than today's Mars holds, so it cannot give the 35 to 40 K of warming the climate needs. The warming comes from super-greenhouse gases, perfluorocarbons at tens of ppm made by the Republic's PFC plants (after McKay, Toon and Kasting 1991 and Marinova, McKay and Hashimoto 2005), helped by the pressure broadening of 52 kPa of N₂ and O₂. If the plants stopped, the PFCs would decay over centuries, not years: a political lever, not a cliff (SETTING §4.5).
  - The air column (P/g ≈ 1,400 g/cm² at datum, 1,770 at Melas) shields the surface from radiation better than Earth's does. The cold is real (mean −25 °C, equatorial lowland days up to +15 °C, nights −30 °C), and so are the dust storms.
- **Gravity:**

  | Place | Surface gravity | Day |
  |---|---|---|
  | Mars | 0.380 G | a sol is 24 h 39 m 35 s (88,775,244 ms) |
  | Europa | 0.134 G | 85 h, locked to Jupiter |
  | Callisto | 0.126 G | |
  | Ganymede | 0.146 G | |
  | Ceres | 0.029 G | |
  | Phobos | about 0.0006 G (effectively free fall) | |
  - Ships under thrust have deck gravity equal to the thrust, pointing aft. Coasting ships are in free fall.
  - Spin gravity exists only where LONGHAUL spins things (spun stations). The rest is thrust, ground or nothing.
- **The Corps:**
  - **The MCRN Marine Corps** is the Navy's ground, boarding and shipboard-security arm.
  - Enlisted and officer ranks follow the real-world US Marine model: Private, Private First Class, Lance Corporal, Corporal, Sergeant, Staff Sergeant, Gunnery Sergeant, First Sergeant/Master Sergeant, Sergeant Major/Master Gunnery Sergeant; then 2nd Lieutenant to General.
  - Units run fire team (4), squad (3 teams + leader = 13), platoon (3 squads + HQ, 44 with its corpsman and radio operator), company (191 with attachments: PEOPLE §3.2, CONTRACTS §15), battalion (about 1,000; 2/4 deploys 980).
  - Martian Marines train at 1 G under thrust and in centrifuges to be fit to fight Earthers and to ride warships. **G-conditioning** is a core stat (see Body).
- **The player's home unit:**
  - 1st Squad, 2nd Platoon, Echo Company, 2nd Battalion, 4th Marine Regiment.
  - The base is **Marine Corps Base Melas**, on the floor of Melas Chasma in the Mariner Valley (about −4.5 km, about 66 kPa, breathable). The nearest town is **Redwater**, one of LONGHAUL's names.
  - Units and names are OLYMPUS's own; no canon units or characters.
- **The Jovian theatre** is set from LONGHAUL's start state.
  - Jovian space is MCR-held. The UN contests Europa and Ganymede.
  - Europa's ground control is OPA ("Ice Control"; airspaces Conamara, Pwyll, Thera). A few million live in ice miners' towns on and under the shell. OPA influence of 0.30 is above the insurgency threshold.
  - **Callisto** hosts the MCRN's Jovian base.
  - **The peacekeeping mission** is a Martian stabilisation force on Europa under a Jovian accord. It guards checkpoints and the water and power works, keeps the peace between the miners' unions, the ice-town authorities, corporate security and Ceres Free Belt cells, and watches UN observers.
  - Europa's surface takes about 5.4 Sv/day of radiation from Jupiter's belts, so the towns live under the ice and surface work is timed by dosimeter.
- **Money:** LONGHAUL's credit, written "MCR". Pay is by rank and comes every 30 days on the ship's or base's UTC calendar. Spend it at the PX, in town, on leave, and on gear you may buy.
- **Epoch:** OLYMPUS starts in 2351, on LONGHAUL's timeline and ephemeris. The design section SETTING.md sets the start date and the Martian calendar (sols).

### Time
- Game time is **integer milliseconds**, ticked at **20 Hz (TICK_MS 50)**, one game second per real second.
  - There are **no speed controls** while you act. Space pauses, and an open overlay pauses.
  - Stop motion runs in shooting mode and in CQC (tradecraft's), and time stands still while you talk. A contact seen at 1:1 stops the world once the marine has taken it in (COMBAT §25.4).
- **Skip-time activities** (eat, shower, toilet, sleep, a training block, cleaning a rifle, reading, a class, a centrifuge session, waiting on watch) jump the clock behind an **illustrated splash**. The splash is the stage itself, zoomed close on you at the appliance, with the action's vignette (steam, the mess tray, the rack's curtain) and a clock running fast. It is skippable to its end, and it is **interruptible** by events (an alarm, a summons, a hostile contact, a need going critical).
  - The world gets **outcomes for the gap**, not a tick-by-tick simulation: needs accrue in closed form, bleeding runs minute by minute, healing runs hourly at daily rates (TIME §11.3), persistent NPCs are placed where their schedule says, and the crowd is recycled.
- **Three clock faces**, all derived from the one game time:
  - **Mars local time:** a 24-hour clock with the 39 m 35 s **timeslip** after midnight (KSR's convention), shown as "Sol 214 · 06:12" plus the Mars year.
  - **UTC (ship time):** used by the Navy, ships, Callisto and Europa's towns.
  - **Your body clock:** circadian, drifts with sleep.
  - The HUD shows only what your character carries: a wrist terminal, a suit HUD, or a wall clock in view (tradecraft's rule).
- **Long stretches** (a voyage, a quiet week) are **chapters**. You choose how to spend each day (train, maintain, study, socialise, rest, volunteer for duty). Key days are lived, and interrupts drop you into real time. Chapters print **papers**: orders, the voyage log, pay, evaluations.
- **The background world** steps in layers:
  - **fast** (20 Hz): the area you're in;
  - **slow** (per minute): off-map persistent people, ship systems and life support, the voyage;
  - **world** (hourly and daily): politics, fleets, the war, the news.
  - Far things get outcomes, not simulation (LONGHAUL's rule).

### Space and scales
- **Close-up play** is the **pixel stage**, tradecraft's look: an orthographic camera looking north, tilted 45° (the user calls this "top-down"), pixel art drawn by code, a generated palette with every pixel snapped to it, outlines, toon light, real lamp pools and darkness, and cutaway interiors.
  - **The look is 16 px/m at 640×360 with chibi figures (2.4 heads), the user's pick from the scored look round** (2026-11-06: every star on 16 px/m chibi; the squad bay scored 80 for it, 65 for 24 px/m chibi, 55 for 24 px/m soldier, 30 for 16 px/m soldier). Everything takes `PX_PER_M` from one constant. The soldier figure's templates are deleted, so no frame is drawn twice; the gallery keeps its shots and scores (VIEWS §2.3). At 16 px/m a magazine or a pistol is drawn bigger than life where it must read, and the weapon card carries what the sprite cannot (VIEWS §2.2).
  - A **close figure** at double density (tradecraft's doll) shows your body and kit (inventory, wounds, suit) and gives the splash vignettes.
- **Ship interiors are stacks of decks along the thrust axis.** The view looks down a deck toward the stern, which is "down" under thrust. Ladders and lifts join the decks; one deck shows at a time with its walls cut away.
- **Higher scales are honest instruments** drawn in LONGHAUL's grammar: dark, hairline and avionics colours, Canvas2D.
  - **Tactical screen** (local space, km).
  - **Planetary space** (orbits, G contours, airspaces).
  - **System map** (AU, light lag).
  - **Telescope and viewports** (scope-sky: worlds and stars drawn by code, phases).
  - The marine sees these on real screens: a troopship's repeater in the berthing, a terminal, a suit HUD, a drop ship's seat display. Each sits inside a pixel-art bezel at the stage's scale.
  - What they show is only what that screen could know.
- **Areas.** The world is made of **sites** (Base Melas, a troopship, Camp Lofn on Callisto, Conamara Under-Ice, a derelict, …). Sites are made of **areas**: a deck, a building's floor, an outdoor chunk.
  - One area is live at a time, and outdoor chunks stream around you.
  - Persistent people elsewhere are tracked by area and room on their schedules.
  - **The world is handcrafted; what happens in it is procedural** (the user, 2026-11-05: "game world is handcrafted, events and world politics, assignments, opportunities, mission details are all procedural").
    - **Handcrafted:** every site, area, building, room, ship deck, town and stretch of ground is authored, placed by hand and the same in every career, so the player learns Base Melas, *Testudo*'s decks and Pwyll Deep as places. Large grounds (the Talus, Europa's surface) may be laid with tools while they are authored, then committed as fixed data and finished by hand: nothing in a place's layout or dressing is rolled per career (my call on the method). "At least three kinds of everything" holds as authored variety: three hand-made squad bays, not three rolls of one.
    - **Procedural, from the world's seed and the career's choices:** events and incidents, world politics, fleets and the war (LONGHAUL's sim), the news, assignments and orders, opportunities (schools, details, volunteer duties, selection windows), mission details (objectives, the opposition, intel, timings, where in a handcrafted place things happen and who is there), and **every person**: the persistent cast and the crowd alike (below, People).

### Bodies, weapons, skills
- Physiology, needs, wounds and medicine are ported from tradecraft (health, medicine, aid, diagnosis, armour) and extended. New systems: thirst, hygiene, bladder and bowel, air (O₂, CO₂, pressure), temperature, G-load (acute and chronic), radiation dose, deconditioning versus **G-conditioning**, and suit systems. LONGHAUL's G thresholds and juice are re-based to seconds.
- **Weapons** run on tradecraft's firearms engine (state machine, timed steps, fumbles, muzzle discipline, malfunctions), with a Martian arsenal:
  - **Hecate Arms & Drive** builds most of it, with other makers for variety;
  - named calibres;
  - **shipboard frangible loads versus ground ball and AP**;
  - recoil, brass and movement modelled in low g and zero g.
  - Shot resolution is retuned for military ranges. Every modifier is still in the log.
  - **Worn combat kit is a superpower's** (the user, 2026-11-07: "standard issue combat gear will be combat EVA suits with serious targetting and filtering, night vision, I mean this is superpower level military"): the Corps' and its peers' line troops wear combat EVA suits (a filtered visor, sound filtering, night vision, targeting), so a flash or a bang does not blind or deafen them; **power armour** (extreme ballistic protection, servo- and computer-guided targeting, built-in automatic weapons) is a special unit's kit class, not common, not built (COMBAT §7.9, §11.4, WEAPONS §3, ROADMAP §4.2, §5.8).
- **Skills and checks** are tradecraft's: d20 + skill + named modifiers against DC, no attributes, a class over-skill (**Warfighting** for soldiers), logarithmic growth, training hours, rust, and fitness with effective fitness.

### People
- **Tiers:**
  1. **Your people:** your squad and chain of command, full depth.
  2. **Your unit:** the platoon and company, a full sheet and a schedule, simulated at lower detail off-map.
  3. **Named people of a place:** base staff, a town's notables.
  4. **The crowd:** seeded, throwaway, recycled after skips, never popping in view.
- **Every person is generated** (the user, 2026-11-05, asked whether the persistent cast should be handcrafted: "All people generated"). Your squad, your NCOs and officers and the key people of each site are rolled from the world's seed (names, faces, backgrounds, traits, ties and arcs drawn from authored pools), so each career meets new people; nobody is a written character. Only places are handcrafted.
- **Every persistent person** has a schedule (the duty roster and the Plan of the Day), needs, moods, the tradecraft fear/trust/affinity gauges toward you and toward each other, memories of events, a career, and story arcs.
- **The chain of command is live.** Orders are given and expected, and so are customs (salutes outdoors, reporting, asking permission), discipline (counselling, extra duty, NJP) and praise.

### Controls (tradecraft's, read by physical key and labelled AZERTY)
- ZQSD walk; Shift run; Alt stroll; E held looks, tapped glances back.
- F: people. R: what's at hand. T: the instinct's line (the drill instructor's voice in your head, dry). In the field, your NCO's voice on comms joins it. What T does grows with training: one step a press for the untrained, a whole drill for the trained, never a shot (COMBAT §25.8).
- G: weapon ready, which means intent and starts stop motion. X: shooting mode. Digits pick steps.
- K L M: hit, grab, guard.
- I inventory, C character sheet, H first aid, Tab the mind (agenda, the known map, people), J log, Space pause, ? keys, Esc menu. The mouse is for menus and aiming.
- Military actions go in the step panel where they fit. Eight that are frequent outside the weapon mode get keys of their own (Claude's call, VIEWS §10): A stance up and W stance down (physical KeyQ and KeyZ), V carry posture, U the suit, N the light, B the radio, O orders and signals, P the terminal. Inside the weapon mode they are also steps.

### Saves and death
- IndexedDB (`olympus`), plain JSON state with `$` runtime keys stripped, `SAVE_SCHEMA` with additive `upgradeState`, and export and import of JSON files.
- Ironman or Standard is chosen per career.
- The world's timeline continues past your death, and the next recruit can enlist into the same world. The memorial keeps the dead.

### Stack and code (see TECH.md)
- **Browser, vanilla ES modules, no build step, fully offline.**
  - three.js 0.186.1 is vendored (MIT) for the pixel stage only.
  - Canvas2D draws the instruments. The UI is plain DOM over the canvas.
  - Saves go to IndexedDB.
- **The sim is pure** (`src/sim`, no DOM). It reports facts on a bus, and the UI decides how to show them.
- **Fixed tick order.** All randomness comes through one seeded rng with streams; cosmetic choices come from hashes.
- **Units** go in constant names (`rangeM`, `speedMs`, `doseSv`).
- **Every module** opens with a prose header that explains its model.
- **One quantity, one number, one helper,** shared by every screen.
- **Debug handle:** `window.__olympus` exposes state, step(n), start, newGame, save, skip and pause. URL parameters skip the title for tests and shots.
- **Tests:** `tests/*.test.mjs`, run by `node tests/run.mjs [filter]`. QUICK leaves out the heavy tests; browser tests use playwright-core and the system Chrome. Tests print the numbers that matter.
- **Performance:**
  - 60 fps on the user's Mac and on Windows (ANGLE/D3D11);
  - no texture uploads mid-play that hitch;
  - static geometry batched;
  - one instanced draw for the crowd;
  - sim under 2 ms a tick in the busiest area.

### Method (see CLAUDE.md)
- The play copy is `.play/`, a worktree pinned to a release tag, served on :8200 and moved only by `tools/promote.sh` on the user's go. The dev tree is served on :8201.
- Releases go through `tools/release.sh`: CHANGELOG, VERSION, tests, commit, tag, push to GitHub.
- Each milestone ends with the user's acceptance moments. Every user complaint goes into the design as a dated rule.

## Sections (docs/design/)

| Section | What it settles |
|---|---|
| [SETTING.md](design/SETTING.md) | The world in 2351 for a marine; Mars (air by altitude, places, Base Melas, Redwater); the MCRN Marine Corps (organisation, ranks, MOS, customs, pay); the Jovian theatre and the Europa mission; Callisto, Phobos; factions and their faces; the calendar and start date |
| [TIME.md](design/TIME.md) | Clocks and calendars; the 1:1 rules; skip-time activities and the splash; interrupts; chapters and papers; the layered background sim; voyages |
| [BODY.md](design/BODY.md) | Needs and their rates; air, water, heat, radiation; G-load acute and chronic, G-conditioning, deconditioning, juice; sleep and the body clock; wounds, medicine and healing; suits; death |
| [SKILLS.md](design/SKILLS.md) | The skill list, checks, Warfighting, growth, training, rust, NPC skill levels, the instinct |
| [WEAPONS.md](design/WEAPONS.md) | The arsenal and calibres; the gun state machine; handling steps; shooting resolution at military ranges; low-g and zero-g; muzzle discipline and carry posture; the armoury, cleaning, qualification |
| [COMBAT.md](design/COMBAT.md) | Ground combat (stop motion, cover, suppression, the squad, orders); CQC (with suits and low g); boarding and EVA in zero g; ship combat from inside a strapped-in body; casualties and medevac |
| [PEOPLE.md](design/PEOPLE.md) | NPC tiers and architecture; schedules; needs; moods and gauges; memory and gossip; conversation; the chain of command and discipline; crowds; performance |
| [CAREER.md](design/CAREER.md) | Enlisting and the start; promotion; MOS and schools; deployments; reconnaissance and special operations; pay, leave, letters home; injury, discharge, retirement, death, the memorial; Ironman and Standard |
| [PLACES.md](design/PLACES.md) | Sites and areas; Base Melas in full; the troopship and other hulls' deck programs; Callisto; Europa's under-ice towns; special-operations settings; generation and variety |
| [VIEWS.md](design/VIEWS.md) | The pixel stage (look, density, camera, light, cutaways); the splash; the instruments (tactical, planetary, system, telescope); the HUD, sheets and menus; controls in full; audio |
| [TECH.md](design/TECH.md) | Module layout; the tick and the layers; state and saves; the renderer and its performance; what is ported from LONGHAUL and tradecraft; testing and tooling |
| [ROADMAP.md](design/ROADMAP.md) | Milestones from 0.0.x to 1.0, each with its acceptance moments; what is built when, in which order, and why |
| [CONTRACTS.md](design/CONTRACTS.md) | The shared contracts in one place: time constants, the start, the Plan of the Day, job slots, budgets, people per scene, state keys, ids, bus facts, save slots, voyages, unit sizes, the air, names. Cited by every section, never restated |

When two documents disagree, this hub wins, then TECH §1.3 (the implementation) and ROADMAP §3.4 (the milestones), then the owner CONTRACTS names, then the rest (my call).

## Decision log

Newest first. Every decision the user makes and every complaint they raise goes here with its date, then into the section it affects.

  - **What the user's reports of 2026-11-19 became** (the carried round and the burn polish, entered in full below): the anchor course explains itself, the hostage card, the walk on mag boots, the target ground by distance, no skip over a surprise, and the burn's strength shown, the boots' 1.0 G limit, a 3 G manoeuvre on every seed, the strap-in drawn. **The play review of the user's test-burn score found three faults in the polish itself, fixed the same round**: the NCO drawn standing by rack 1 while strapped (the view's memory froze him mid-step), you drawn almost invisible under the checkerboard, and the booted marine thrown by the 3 G jink barely moving, drawn upright, who locked his boots again against the bulkhead 0.45 s later and rode the rest (the user's own complaint again): he now pitches toward the push and his boots refuse to lock while the push across the deck is over `BOOTS_SHEAR_G`. **The code review's**: Strap in done standing beside the bunk (the act is lost the moment you are not lying in the berth), the boots meter and the 1MC on a different rule from the sim (one rule, `bootsHold`), a save at a pause parting on T (the instinct settles its line from what fires when the game has not ticked), `wreckDeckAt` the one deck test.
  - **Rejected by the fixer, said, for the lead** (each with its reason): the trunk transit as a 0.25 s jump with nothing drawn between the decks is the design (F135; a head-first pass and the #2's grip is a new look, to be sampled first); deck against deckhead at 16 px/m waits on `wreck-faces` K; station 2 offering an aim at the cadre is the planner's rule everywhere (marked "! a person: the range stops"); "the top wall" in stations 2 and 3 is said on purpose (`courses.js`' header); the "AIM 1.9 S" plate under the target card in Well A is held, a lead's call on a cosmetic overlap; nothing shows when a pad-eye is in reach for L (a new look element, samples first); "0.00 G ↓ jink to port BOOTS WILL NOT HOLD" for one sample at the onset is right (the ramp starts at 0 on the onset's tick). `skipBarred` in a long profile is a design note for TIME: in a career an hours-off surprise would bar sleep until its onset, so the rule should then read a horizon.

  - **The strength shown.** The 1MC's warning names each event's size and, for a push across the deck, its direction, "Brace, brace, brace. Secure for burn: one G. Burn in fifteen seconds." and "Secure for manoeuvring: three G to port. Manoeuvre in ten seconds. Strap in: boots will not hold three G." (the last sentence wherever locked boots will not hold it), and says it again as the change comes in, "Burning. One G.", "Jinking. Three G to port.", a surprise's too (`src/data/onemc.js` `warnWords` with the event, `onsetWords`, `gWords`, `dirOf`; text bubbles, as every line). The G line under the watch gives the size and an arrow, "BRACE 0:08 · JINK 3.0 G ↓ PORT" through the warning and "3.00 G ↓ jink to port" or "1.00 G ▼ burn" while it acts (v0.0.24 showed "0.00 G · jink" through a jink: the felt g, not the push), and a G meter under it (0 to 3 G, the push now, the coming size hatched through the warning, a mark at what boots hold) with "BOOTS WILL NOT HOLD" in red beside it, "STRAPPED IN" in green once you are (`gravity.js` `gLineOf`'s `toG`, `dir`, `pushG`, `holds`; `strip.js` `gMeterOf`). The siren whoops twice as fast for a manoeuvre's warning as for a burn's (`siren.js` `cycleMsOf`). (my calls: the arrow is the screen's way the push throws a body, port the berth's +z with forward west; ▼ a felt change onto the deck)
  - **The boots' limit.** Mag boots hold a push along the deck's normal by v0.0.24's rule (`BRACE_G` 1.5 G warned, `SURPRISE_G` 0.5 G a surprise) and a push across the deck only up to `GRAV_BODY.BOOTS_SHEAR_G` 1.0 G: the boots break away at `MAG_BOOTS.BREAKAWAY_N` 800 N, 1.02 G on a marine and his utilities of about 80 kg (my call, named 1.0). Over it the boots tear off the plate (logged `torn`) and the body is thrown as an unsecured one, floating across the compartment until it meets a face (freefall.js stops it, impact.js's bands), logged `thrown` at the speed it had. A jink also pulls a body off a hold into the compartment (v0.0.24 put it down prone on a deck that does not hold it at 0 G). Only a body strapped into a rack or a couch rides a hard jink.
  - **The scene's manoeuvre.** test-burn gains a warned evasive manoeuvre after the burn on every seed, 3 G to port for 2 s, in at 3 G/s, its onset 95 to 100 s in, warned 10 s before: the burn teaches that boots or the rack will do, the manoeuvre that only the rack will (my call, the user's "3G sideways"). Listed third, so the surprise keeps its draw (the same seeds have it); the surprise moved to 115 to 130 s, after it. The NCO calls it ("Racks! Boots will not hold three G sideways. Strap in!") and straps himself into rack 1; the T line from its warning to its onset says the boots' limit. The NCO stands at (1.5, 2.55), off your rack's foot (you stood in him to strap in). The card and the summary say the manoeuvre in plain words, how you were secured as it came in, the boots torn off and the speed you met the compartment's side at, and never the footing's "couched". Re-pinned: `env-noregress`'s test-burn digest (1881687a385edc to 1368bf34d3e065, the cause on the line).
  - **Strapped in drawn.** Strap in (R at your rack's foot, 2.0 s) lies you down in your rack's lower berth in its first 1.2 s (`RACK_IN_MS`, the barracks' lying down's frames, `place.js` `lieActor`) and buckles the straps in the rest; strapped, you lie on the made rack in your utilities with its three straps across you, chest, hips and knees, a buckle each and a ring at each rail (`bedding.js`, `straps`), and the doll wears the harness (the gear's anchor `straps`, `worn.js`). Unstrap (2.0 s, 1.0 s before) undoes the straps and gets you out to the foot in its last 1.2 s (`RACK_OUT_MS`). A strapped body never rises by a move key or T (`riseActor`), and the barracks' "Up. The rack will still be here tonight." is quiet aboard. (my calls: the times; no source draws a transport's rack harness)

The course now explains itself to a player who has never floated: the cadre's brief is followed once by the three ideas (from rest the move keys push you off the deck, a wall or a hold in reach; you drift until you meet something and L grabs a hold; you fire only from what holds you; Alt gentle, Shift hard, G the panel, T your next step), and every station's command says what to press in the game's own terms, the panel row by its label and the key by its AZERTY label, both read from the code (`core/keys.js` label, `arms/shooting.js` LABELS: "Station two, the ring at the top wall.   - **Free fall** (B; my calls): the push's speed ramps over its step; the hands' 1.5 m/s and the other limbs' 0.5 times in impacts; the bone index 1 until BODY's daily job is built; the drift and tumble are a floating body's only (an anchor keeps its share); no drag, a face met stops the body (no rebound); the keys have no sprint push (2.0 m/s is a step's); a grabbed person shares the drift; a ground in free fall's cases meets the nav window's edge (a test parameter's only). **The review's**: a floating body's height is `actor.floatY`, a field of its own (the centre of mass over the deck, `FREEFALL.FLOAT_Y_M` 1.0 m), never `actor.y`, which stays the floor a body stands or lies on and which the hit model and the blast read as the feet; two bodies drifting as one (a holder at the grab's offset, `actor.buddyOff`, the man held drifting by the shared momentum on the holder's tick).
  - **Gravity events** (C; my calls): the fall integrates the ramp, so **F112's 7.67 m/s is the sharp onset's** (20 G/s: the body lands after the ramp, the square root of 2 a y); the ordinary 0.5 G/s ramp lands a body thrown from 3 m up at **5.83 m/s after 1.54 s**, before the ramp is done: the gauge is read at the sharp onset, the ramp's figure printed beside it, and no number was tuned (a numerical integration agrees to 0.001); the impact is written on the tick the body lands; a jink is lateral and never changes the felt g; the surprise's card is the profile's, out of stop motion; the siren plays through the warning only, once a second, the 1MC's words once; events run one after another; `DOWN_DROP_M` 0.25 m; a hold keeps to 1 G; `profile.js` is apart from `gravity.js` because two import cycles broke the load otherwise. **The review's**: a body resting on a fixture (`restsOn`: lying or sitting) is couched at any change of thrust and reads 'couched' under 0.05 G, only free fall's height is fallen from (13 sleepers fell before), and the catwalk's instructor is judged standing; the free card keeps no answer, so it has no "R keep this answer" line.

- 2026-11-19, the user's gallery picks (the gallery built 2026-11-16 18:13 UTC; "Lots of stale picks since I failed to review them earlier, most of what is already in game is fine, I did comment on some choices to replace what is in game though"). The cuts, the guns (G2 as the R-7 with V1's sight, G1, V1, M1, G0 purged, the finishes) and the hit card B were decided on 2026-11-08 (below) and are repeated unchanged. New, with the notes verbatim, built in v0.0.24's role F unless said:
  - **The shot** (A ★ A4): "If i had to pick only one it's the last one, however, I would use the R-7 wave for the P-12 and make the R-7 sound meaner, blunter, more bass heavy. less a "PAF" and more a "DOOM" if that makes sense." **Decided**: A4 in every scene; the P-12 fires today's R-7 wave; the R-7 is rebuilt meaner, blunter and heavier in the bass, shipped as the default and sent as a sample to score (my call).
  - **The voices**: "forget about voices, those are all terrible use text bubble only". **Decided**: no voiced lines by default; every line is shown as a text bubble at its speaker (the instructor, your #2, the role-players, the ship's 1MC); the gunshot, the steps and the siren are sounds and stay. The user again, the same day: "also let me reiterate : forget voice synthesis, no voices." **Decided**: v0.0.23 ships with no line voiced (the default off, the lines as text); v0.0.24 (F) removes the voice synthesis whole (`src/data/voices.js`, `src/ui/audio/voice.js`, the engine's bake, `&voice=`, `samples/voices`, the To score page's voice row, the tests on them retired with the reason), and draws every line as a text bubble at its speaker. No role adds a voiced line again.
  - **The helmets**: R: "You can use the first one (current one) but for civilian vac suits not military. R2, R3 and R4 are very good for military, R1 is redundant with R3" (R-d ★ 90). E: "Torn with the first one, very good in game but the card is not good the mask laks polish and love. In fact it's the same for all of them, they lok good on the chibi characters, but the inventory card needs a lot of work and refinement. We can work with this for the time being but it will need to be refined" (E-a ★ 95). O: "O2 is excellent, even the card". The trim: "So much better with the trim ! It's decided, trim for all :D, even the cards are much better with it". **Decided**: S0 (today's sealed helmet) for civilian vac suits only; the Corps' regulars R3 by default, R2 and R4 the variants by unit, R1 dropped (my call on R3, the best scored); elites E1; the OPA O2; faction trim on every helmet (Mars red, the UN's blue), figure and card. The I card's masks are to be refined later (the overhaul's third round).
  - **The held devices**: G: "Use the ones that make sense depending on how far we're throwing, those are all good" (G1 at the chest, G2 low at the hip, G3 cocked at the shoulder, chosen by the throw's distance); B3, R2, C1, K1, H1, P1 and the chemlight M2 as in game. P: "Good, but don't overthink this, keep things simple, it's a game. I love the attention do detail, but only if it won't impact gameplay or performance (or introduce so much complexity it creates bugs)": **a standing rule** for every look.
  - **The rifle held**: R7: "Rifles are not worn on the back when slung they are hanging front, I believe that's what's in game right now anyway. The Doll is wrong, nobody holds their gun like that"; M (the live men): "Already commented on the stance of rifle aiming = NOT GOOD, M2 and M3 are decent masks though". **Decided**: the slung rifle hangs in front (as in game); the doll's rifle hold redrawn as a marine holds it (E, the doll's owner); the figure's aimed rifle (the live men and you) redrawn: stock in the shoulder, cheek on the stock, support hand on the handguard (F); the live men's masks M2 and M3.
  - **The P-12**: "whatever is in game is perfect" (A); P12C: "gosh this one as an alt is very good as well"; P12D: "add this for variety as well, don't forget there will be many more weapons as we build variety later on"; P12B (the slab) 0/100, dropped. P12M: "The suit needs more color variation for the gloves, they dont stand out enough (maybe gray gloves would be better for this color suit), because the gun looks better in the hands of the unsuited guy." **Decided**: C and D kept as alternate pistols' assets; the combat EVA suit's gloves grey.
  - **The walk** (W): "W-c for sure": W-c is cycle B ("B, eight frames, rolling", VIEWS §3.10.4), the game's since v0.0.17, so B stays the game's walk.
  - **The rest**: the look stays chibi at 16 px/m; the panel stays P3 ("That idea is probably the best proposal if we can make the controls work cleanly"); the target view (T): "just pick what you will, I'll revise what I find weird in game, use what makes sense again depending on distance" (E's v0.0.24 item, by distance, my call); H2: "whatever you have in game is fine, except that we shouldn't know what's behind closed doors or walls without looking" (v0.0.23's unseen rooms); WC2-A and P12A as in game. "R: No image, I can't rate a description": a set was shown without its image; F finds it and reshoots it.
  - **The gallery**: "please in the future, place the already rated picks in a hidden drop down section at the bottom". **Decided**: the gallery and the To score page put every set already rated in a collapsed section at the bottom, the waiting ones on top, and "Copy my picks" gives only the picks made since the last copy (the count of the earlier ones in one line).
- 2026-11-19, v0.0.23, "The portrait is what you would see: the gun in his hands, the man as he stands, the rooms you have not seen, and the first hour through the door" (the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.23.md`; the visual overhaul's first round, on the user's notes of 2026-11-17 above and "no voices" of 2026-11-19). Stage 0 (the lead), then A (the record and the back of a man), B (the stage's knowledge), E1 (the samples on today's code), C (the portraits and the P-12's stances), E2 (the samples on C's pose engine) and D (the carried fixes), merged in that order on v0.0.22's tag, then the integration (`.run/v023/integrate.md`), a play review, a code review and a fixer (`.run/v023/fix.md`). The lead's six calls and the integration's own are in the entry below; the roles' and the fixer's follow. **The user's notes, as built** (VIEWS §2.11, §3.10, §4.4, §7.7, §7.11; COMBAT §25.10, §25.11, §25.19.7, §25.23; PEOPLE §7.6; TECH §2.2, §13.9; CONTRACTS §26): the portrait is the man as you see him (one record, `seenOf`, read from the stage's own readers; the gun in his hands, his pose, his facing from your eye, a man's back); nobody is drawn before he is seen, and a drawn house is black inside until you see into it; the P-12 has the rifle's stances and the slung R-7 hangs on its sling; "Look behind the door" and "Watch the near right corner" say what they do; no line is voiced.
  - **The record** (A; my calls, the code decides where it differs from the sheet): the role-players' frames come from `rangeLook().house.men`, not `opforLook` (which no stage reader calls); three rows of `FRAME_SEEN` corrected to what the stage draws (a man aiming from the knee is drawn kneeling; a man giving up has the rifle lowered in his hands, not up; `prone` added for a shooter down on the line); `'pistol-high'` joins `HOLD_HOWS` (the stage-0 lead's call for the pistol note: the P-12's high ready was folded into its low ready); `hold.act` (`draw`, `holster`, `check`, `reload`, `clear`) is the P-12's only, so a rifle's admin does not repaint the card on every charge; a magazine in a hand is `how 'mag'` only with no gun in the hands; `SEEN_KEYS` adds `kind` and `face` (a board's threat and no-shoot share their gear); the viewer's eye is the viewer actor's point, not `eyeOf()` (which also computes light and view); `rangeLook` is made once a tick (a `WeakMap` by state, tick and ms; `{ fresh: true }` for a caller that changes a man between reads); `back.js` draws from the waist up, the legs and boots stay `doll.js`'s; F86's recorded set is committed as `tests/seen-lookkeys.json` (the tests cannot read `.run/`), re-recorded by the lead when a stage change is meant; `seen-parity` is split in two files and runs one lane quick (the others in its heavy case: 4 lanes took 6.0 s of CPU against 3.3).
  - **The stage's knowledge** (B; my calls): the named layouts B and C are familiar (briefed as lane A is: the instructors' panel settings); the catwalk and the booth are known from the start; a ring of one cell round what you see (a wall's own cell is never in sight); the known cells are bits on the GPU in a texture of their own (the fog's 48 KB mask keeps its format; 2,240 B go only when a cell turns known); your #2 and the instructor are never hidden (the combat rule hid the instructor on 5 frames); a man whose remembered spot is in sight while he is not is hidden until he is in sight again, never drawn where he truly is in the dim; the sim keeps no durable "ever seen" record (a new saved field would move every pin's saves), so a man glimpsed and forgotten waits for his next sighting after a load, and the rooms you entered count on a new state's first frame only.
  - **The portraits** (C; my calls): the holster's side is mirrored from behind (from behind his right thigh's holster is at the viewer's right); the low ready is drawn as v0.0.22 drew it and the other stances a step lighter over the fists; RH1 hangs on the support side (clear of the pistol on the middle line); the aimed-away holds are warmed at the near band only (46 dolls inside `DOLL_LRU` 48); prone is always end on (Y-p); the gun on the doll's card is read by `pm.held` (the hands' own part set), never by colour; the walk is four keys a cycle across both steps and the aimed walk the turret's arms over the same legs, so the rifle pool keeps its slot counts (`RIFLE_SHEET_W_M` 85); the aim's side is held 300 ms on the card (`AIM_DWELL_MS`, a side held but never a raise, a lower, a kneel or a turn; I did not ask A for hysteresis in the record); `poses.js` and `compose.js` were given to C by the pistol note (stage 0's call).

As built, the fog of awareness (VIEWS §2.11) only dims what you cannot see: the room's walls, its furniture and every man in it are drawn darker but drawn, and `render/memory.js` draws a man never yet seen where he stands. **To build (my calls, with the visual overhaul, v0.0.23)**: a place you know (your barracks, the range, a house's plan you were briefed on) keeps today's dimmed view; in a place you do not know (the shoot house as drawn, every unfamiliar interior from then on), a cell never seen is not drawn at all (black, or the brief's outline if the brief gave one), and is revealed by sight and then remembered, its walls and things as last seen; a person or a thing is drawn only where it is seen now, or as last seen, never before it has been seen (a sound or a "felt" presence shows as the fog's felt tint, never as a figure). The plan view and the debrief after the lane still show the whole house. One rule in the view, read from perception's visibility and a place's familiarity, so the dice and the picture agree.

From the corporal up the drill looks there by itself; below, the row is yours and the DI names it ("Behind the door. Then: "same for the watch the near right corner etc". **Watch: <spot>** is the same hot spot from outside the room, at the stack or in the doorway: it brings your muzzle up (high ready) onto that corner through the door and holds it there while you walk in by the keys, so a man who appears there meets your muzzle already on him (the take-in starts pre-armed and your feet carry on); **Look: <spot>** is the same check once inside, the eyes onto the spot to mark it covered. **To build (my call)**: both rows name their action and purpose ("Watch the near right corner: muzzle on it as you go in"; "Look: check the near right corner"), the spot is drawn on the floor when the row is under the cursor, and the panel's group says "outside: aim in; inside: check".

(5) **A drawn lane door's lock is unknown until read or tried; the read takes the lock; the par books the try** (COMBAT §25.19.2.1, PLACES §1.6). (6) **A stand's par gains `turn`**, the muzzle's turn off its owner's sector over `TURN_FREE_DEG` 1° at `AIM.turn`'s rate (threats and armoured men; the hostage's head has its own), and a second man of the same corner in a window times his par from the first man's first round plus that man's booked row (COMBAT §25.10.4's pars); the set gains none. (7) **The holster at Stop rides on MAKE SAFE through `sidearm.js` `ADMIN`** (the P-12's share of the tower's macros), never in macros.js; the cold P-12 is the tower's move-on's, said and noted, never a new wait (WEAPONS §5.3, §5.4). (11) **The 2 ms rule is the minimum of three runs of each tick**, 4 ms on a minute boundary, the mean printed (TECH §16.1; question 14's default); **the quick suite's wall limit is scaled by 7 / the workers and each file has a CPU budget** (TECH §13.7); the sum's budget is `QUICK_CPU_S` 375 × k, not the sheet's 200, because this Mac's quick run measures 288 to 446 s of CPU and a bound under the machine's own cost would only ever fail. (13) **The acceptance list's statuses are WIRED, OPEN, USER, ANSWERED, LATER and GAP, never "met"**: a green suite is the suites' run, and a gauge the last heavy run left red is OPEN with its numbers (today F70, F71, F73). The stall alone had no upper bound: the fuzz private on 1111 ran to 600 s on v0.0.20's integration. Question 7, paint on the live lane: "Paintball should end if the hit is 'fatal'." A marking round ends the lane only when the live round it stands for would have been fatal to the man it hit, judged by the body's own wound model through the suit and its armour, never by a count of marks; a hit that would not have been fatal is a wound written on the card and the run sheet, and the lane goes on. My calls: one rule for everyone the marking rounds strike (you, your #2, the role-players); a fatal hit on your #2 ends the lane too; "fatal" is defined from what the wound model can say (built with v0.0.20, COMBAT §25.18.7).

- 2026-11-15, the user after the magazine fixes: "I'm a bit worried about the design of the options ? I feel like the reload, top up etc options should simply look at the state of our weapons ? Also, if we have sidearms, they should somehow show up like the main weapon, showing if holstered or no but should have a chamber and all the sequencing the main weapon has ?" **Decided**, both first in v0.0.18, before the target view (my call on the order: the grenades, the breaching charges and the loadout screen add more items with states, and they should be built on this). (1) The handling options are derived from the weapon's believed state, not hand-written per drill. The believed state is the magazine (in, out, in the hand, its count), the chamber, the bolt, any stoppage and the selector. Each action already has its effect on that state (firearms.js's action-state table). From the state now, the panel offers the shortest way to ready to fire (Fix and the reload slot), and the other purposes as other goals: Top up (a fuller magazine, staying ready), keep the empty. Each row is named by what its path does (Magazine in, RELOAD, Top up, Charge, IMMEDIATE ACTION). A test walks every reachable believed state with every pouch state and asserts that a way to fire is offered, or that the panel honestly says there is none, so a dead end can't be built. (2) The sidearm is shown like the rifle: the weapon card shows both guns, each with where it is (slung, in the hands, holstered) and its condition line (selector, chamber, magazine count). - 2026-11-11, the user on the mastery rule's three questions: "good defaults". **Decided**: a natural 20 on timed work is clean (two thirds of the time; SKILLS question 8); the catch starts at skill value 15 with a 10-point margin (question 9); a caught 1 costs a flat 1.5 times the time. The dice were measured fair (352,722 rolls: 1s 4.92%, 20s 5.00%); the rule and the panel's marks (`~` caught, ★ natural 20, ✗ only a costly failure, `!` a stoppage, SURE) are built in the shoot house rework.
- 2026-11-11, Claude's design for the user's natural 1s (my call; designed, to build in the shoot house rework; SKILLS §5.1, §5.11, §5.12, §5.14, §15.2, questions 8 and 9; WEAPONS §5.1, §5.3, §5.4, §7.10; COMBAT §5.8, §25.4.2, §25.6.1, §25.6.3; VIEWS §7.4; CONTRACTS §11, §13). **The cause**: there is no bug in the dice (352,722 d20s counted: 1s 4.92%, 20s 5.00%; the generator alone 5.007% and 4.999%). From the private up, faces 2 to 19 pass, so every miss and fumble was a natural 1, a 20 never changed a result (0 in about 259,000 checks), every routine 1 drew a red ✗ and no 20 drew anything, and one press rolls up to nine dice. **The rule**: a natural 1 is **caught** (a success at ×1.5 of the action's own time, `CATCH_K` 0.5, the user's "50% longer") when it is routine (as before, ×1.6 becoming ×1.5) or **mastered**: value 15 or more (`MASTERY_MIN`) and value + modifiers ≥ DC + 10 (`MASTERY_AT`, the felt chaos counted, the tempo not), at any pressure, never rushed or opposed, for shots, handling and noticing alike, NPCs included. A caught shot leaves late and is judged when it leaves. A natural 20 on timed work is **clean** (×2/3). The panel draws `~` for a caught 1, ★ for a 20, ✗ only for a failure that cost something, `!` for a stoppage (the dummy click), and "sure" where a 1 would be caught. **The numbers**: a round in the shoot house misses 16.9, 5.8, 3.8, 1.4, 0 and 0% (recruit, private, corporal, sergeant, Raider, tier one), against 16.9, 5.8, 5.1, 5.5, 5.6 and 5.7% before; in Pairs 6.8, 4.6, 0.3, 0.3, 0 and 0; under fire in the live room only the Raider and the tier one are spared. The recruit and the private never reach it, so the scored chaos stands. The word is "caught", not "slip", which the game already uses for the Mars timeslip and for a thumb that slips off the lever (WEAPONS §5.4).

- 2026-11-08, the user on the lore round's questions, verbatim: "1: Earth is going to happen much later indeed, however, the systems we build need to allow for procedural generation of scenes and missions on earth the same way as on Europa or Ganymede or some portion of a station under Ceres. We will play in predefined chunks (scenes) that will be generated using basic rules like atmosphere levels, gravity (can include high G maneuvers in some rare cases with alert "brace" sirens on ships mid flight), whether we can use mag boots (metal plating on ships for instance), light levels, etc.. All that to say that the engine needs to support any surface on any part of the system by design. 3: We are but a cog in the machine, there is no such thing as "the other marines", our player character is part of a squad and we have the same stuff. 4: Try keeping rifles looking like rifles even zero G stuff, let's say we use footing to hold recoil". Questions 2 (the J-12's real weakness up close), 5 (the engine-lighting reflex card) and 6 (zero-g rooms cleared by faces) keep their defaults.
  - **Decided, a standing rule for the engine**: any surface in any part of the system is supported by design. Missions are played in predefined chunks (scenes) generated by rules from a scene's parameters: the atmosphere (none to Earth's thick wet air, its pressure and make-up), gravity (from microgravity to 1 G and more, a ship's thrust changing it mid-scene, rare high-G manoeuvres with "brace" sirens on ships in flight), surfaces mag boots hold to (a ship's metal plating) or not, light levels, and the like. Earth comes much later, but nothing built may assume Mars: Earth, Europa, Ganymede and a section of a station under Ceres are scenes of the same engine. This reconciles with 2026-11-05's "game world is handcrafted ... mission details are all procedural": the career's places (bases, ships, stations) are authored; a mission's scene is generated from authored chunks by those rules.
  - **Decided, kit**: the player is "a cog in the machine": there is no "other marines" kit. The player carries what their squad carries; variety in arms comes by unit, role, posting and era, and applies to the player as to anyone (the R-5, the R-7M and the rest are issued, never a cosmetic difference between the player and the squad). WEAPONS question 18 answered so.
  - **Decided, zero-g arms**: rifles stay rifle-shaped, the zero-g ones included; recoil in free fall is held by footing (mag boots, anchoring, bracing), not by launcher-like designs. The V-5X fired over the shoulder is withdrawn (WEAPONS question 19 answered so): a countermass or low-recoil design, if kept, keeps a rifle's form and handling.

- 2026-11-07, the user on the hardening design's questions: "perfect defaults except 6 : This is also a hard scifi game based on futuristic expectations of a couple centuries over, standard issue combat gear will be combat EVA suits with serious targetting and filtering, night vision, I mean this is superpower level military, some marines will even be equipped with power armor (not common, special units) with extreme ballistic protection and servo/computer guided targetting with built-in automatic weapons." **Decided**: the hardening design's questions 1 to 5 and 7 to 11 take their defaults (the name Hardening and its words; the tier one at 95; noise alone never pins a Raider or a tier one; friendly fire close by suppresses; the startle takes the keys; two bangs can break insurgents, one never; "Three bangs" without ear defenders; a turner may turn in the reload on `test-stress` and in the shoot house, not on `test-pairs`; the hit card A, the doll in colour; belief then truth on it). **Question 6 is overruled**: a superpower's standard combat kit is a combat EVA suit with serious targeting, flash and sound filtering and night vision (the Corps' and its peers'), so a flashbang buys little against suited troops (its stun and flash are filtered; what still reaches them is the felt chaos the suit cannot filter) and the bang's window belongs to entries against the unsuited (insurgents, gangs, police without suits, civilians, crews caught out of kit). **Power armour** exists for special units, not common: extreme ballistic protection, servo- and computer-guided targeting, built-in automatic weapons; a kit class of its own (WEAPONS, BODY, COMBAT, ROADMAP to place). Training scenes on the range are in utilities and ear defenders, as now. **Applied** (2026-11-08, v0.0.9): questions 1 to 5 and 7 to 11 are marked answered where they live (COMBAT §24 questions 16 to 20, 22 and 23, and 21's overrule; VIEWS and SKILLS for the words), and question 6's overrule is written into COMBAT §5.1, §7.9, §11.4, §24 and §25.18.2, WEAPONS §3's worn kit classes, BODY §17.3 and ROADMAP §4.2, §5.8 (the build sheet's item 3: protection from worn kit, the UN's line infantry, MSS tactical and enemy special forces suited in `combat-race`; the range scene stays utilities, and `setup=bangs` takes the issue's ear defenders off as the user's case of 'Three bangs' without ear defenders, the default of question 23).
- 2026-11-07, the user on close-quarters accuracy, suppression and hardening, verbatim: "At 10m, almost no trained marine in real life would miss when coming in straight to shoot someone so it's ok that only speed changes. An other way of saying it is "To shoot at close to 100% accuracy, it takes more time to aim for someone with less skill and experience than for a tier one operator". That being said, in the chaos of CQB (noise, suppression from enemy fire and nearby friendly fire, flashbangs etc), performance should drop more sharply for a recruit than a battle hardened over-trained operator who would mostly be unaffected by that. In fact, suppression should be a roll modifier that picks up on those factors, with "hardening" (can name it differently) being an offset to it. Tier one operator would have such hardening, that having three flashbangs go off right next to him would barely affect his ability to operate his weapon, where a recruit would likely be so supressed that they would be even likely to fail simple actions like thumbing safety off or reloading. The whole point of high kinetic action (flashbangs etc) on entry is that the enemy would be highly suppressed as the entry team comes in, giving the entry team serious time to pick every enemy off." And on NPCs: "enemies are NPCs and they run their own procedure to eliminate us, meaning that when we crash in with a lot of noise, they would turn towards us and attempt to shoot us. Suppression, training and exprience will decide how well NPCs can respond to threats and whether they fight back, try to rush for cover or flee all together. Targets in game should be represented with the same logic we represent the shooting range targets, drawn with impact points drawn as well, using similar to inventory and character menu drawings of people."
  - **Decided**: (1) close-range accuracy stays near certain for the trained, skill shows as time (COMBAT §24 question 14 answered: time is enough on `test-pairs`); (2) suppression is a roll modifier fed by the chaos (noise, enemy and friendly fire close by, blasts, flashbangs) and **hardening** offsets it, trained and earned apart from raw skill: a tier one barely touched by three flashbangs beside him, a recruit failing simple actions (the safety, a reload); COMBAT §5's `resist = min(0.6, WF / 150)` does not meet this and is redesigned; (3) kinetic entry exists to suppress the defenders and buy the entry team time; (4) NPCs run their own procedure: on a loud entry they turn to the threat and try to fight, and their suppression, training and experience decide whether they fight back, rush for cover or flee; (5) a person hit is shown as a range target is, the impact points drawn on a figure in the inventory's and PERSONNEL's drawing style (VIEWS §18 question 20 answered). COMBAT §24 question 15 (a turner turning in the lull on `test-pairs`) keeps its default, no; the user's answer applies to NPC enemies.
- 2026-11-07, Claude's design of suppression and hardening for the entry above, merged from two proposals (by systems and by doctrine) as a judge directed, each rule (my call) unless it quotes the user, for the user to overrule (COMBAT §5, §7.10, §7.11, §11.4, §24, §25.18; SKILLS §2.2, §5.3, §5.9, §6.8, §11.1, §15.1; BODY §9.3, §17.3; PEOPLE §3.6, §3.8, §7.2; VIEWS §3.4, §7.3, §7.4, §7.7, §8.2, §11.2, §12, §18; WEAPONS §5.1, §7.3; TECH §2.2; CONTRACTS §9, §11, §13; ROADMAP §4.2, §5.8). The numbers are computed by `/private/tmp/claude-501/olympus-build/hardening/merged.mjs` on the built helpers.
  - **The model** (COMBAT §5). The **pool** is the chaos arriving at a body, 0 to 120, the same for everyone in the same place (rounds cracking close from either side, a friend's muzzle by the ear, shots in the room, a kicked door, blasts, flashbangs, being hit, a friend hit), pooled with saturation; no skill scales an input, only physics does (your own team's called bang ×0.25, forewarned ×0.6, ear defenders ×0.6, vacuum, blanks). **Hardening** H, 0 to 100, is the offset: **felt = clamp(pool − H, 0, 100)**, so the most anyone can feel is 120 − H. Felt is a roll modifier, −1 a 10 points (at most −10) on hands-and-eyes rolls and half on head-and-voice rolls, slows the hands and the aim ×(1 + felt / 200), adds the take-in's built 200 ms a band, and names the band for the words, the nerve check (d20 + Nerve against 1 + round(felt / 5), from felt 45) and the NPC's choice. **Retired**: `resist = min(0.6, WF / 150)` (a tier one beside three bangs was pinned, as a recruit was), the banded −2 to −6, WEAPONS §5.1's flat `fire` −3 (built) and `danger` −1, WEAPONS §7.3's incoming fire −4, "military helmets halve the stun", "stunned 3 to 6 s" and COMBAT §25.4.2's "a pinned tier one 0.65 s".
  - **The startle** (COMBAT §5.7): an input that jumps felt by 10 or more freezes the body for U(1.5, 3) s × (1 − H / 100)², halved per bang within 10 s, the keys taken as in the take-in, stamped "FROZE 1.20 S"; at most about 3 s, so the recruit then tries and fails at his hands, rather than watching. **The flash and the bang are physics**: flash-blind 5 s facing it without a filtered visor, deafened without ear defenders, a concussion grenade's stun for anyone (§11.4).
  - **The anchors** [computed]: three flashbangs beside a **tier one** (H 95) leave him at felt 10, calm, −1, startled one tick, his Off safe and RELOAD failing exactly as often as calm (5%, 14%); beside a **boot recruit** (H 7) at felt 98, pinned, −10, startled 2.45 s, **Off safe failing 55% and a RELOAD 93%**; the private (H 17) fails his RELOAD 56%; from the corporal up only time and the take-in pay. From the turn to the first round after them: the recruit 6.98 s (2.64 calm), the tier one 0.61 s (0.60 calm).
  - **Hardening's two terms, the user's two words** (COMBAT §5.6, SKILLS §6.8): experience, round(WF × HARD_K[class]) (soldier 0.75, three times SKILLS §2.2's `NERVE_K` by class), and training, an inoculation counter 0 to 25 earned by finishing a training run whose chaos reached you (felt 20), at most 4 a sol, capped by its teacher (boot 6, unit 10, the shoot house's cadre 15, schools 20, the Raiders' ITC 25); a real fight teaches through Warfighting only, so it counts once. The body takes some back (exhaustion, a frayed day, a combat stress load over 60, very low morale, a concussion, pain, shock: at most 25). Test sheets: recruit 7, private 17, corporal 36, sergeant 45, Raider 75, tier one 95 (the systems proposal's 3, 12, 25, 35, 70 and 84 kept as the alternative to score). Decay no longer reads the man (4 a second in cover, 2 exposed, 6 staying down, doubled by the new step Breathe). **The three "nerve" words settled**: Nerve is SKILLS' derived check value, unchanged; the nerve axis is PEOPLE's temperament (+3 hardening a step, nothing else); COMBAT §7.10's NPC "nerve" is renamed **resolve**.
  - **NPCs** (COMBAT §7.11): the user's procedure: the cue (the noise turns them to the door: a loud entry buys suppression and sells surprise), the shock and the startle, the turn and the rifle back onto the door, the take-in, then **fight, cover, blind fire, freeze, flee or surrender** by felt band, the nerve check and their doctrine; calm or under fire they fight with no roll; **a choice holds** until the felt band changes or a 1.5 s dwell passes in cover (re-rolled every 0.5 s, an insurgent fled 89% of the time within 10 s of two bangs; with the dwell 29%). Read from the figure's pose (four new sets: daze, cower, blind, hands), the plaque's word and the instinct line, never a number.
  - **The entry** [computed] (COMBAT §25.18.2): against a man waiting at the door without a filtered visor the team's bang buys **about 4 s** (an aimed insurgent's first round 0.75 → 5.15 s; a private by the keys fires first 0% → 100%, against an aimed UN Marine 0% → 80%); the window closes with the flash's 5 s ("On the bang! Go!", the fault "BANG TO ENTRY" past 2 s); against the visored (police tactical teams, special forces) and against anyone not waiting it is a doorbell. On the built helpers two tick-close cells of COMBAT §25.5.5's race move (the tier one against an aimed insurgent 53% not 100%; the sergeant against an aimed UN Marine 47% not 3%): `combat-race` settles that table from the code first, before any constant here is tuned.
  - **The hit card** (VIEWS §7.7, the user's "Targets in game should be represented with the same logic we represent the shooting range targets"): a person hit is drawn in `PAPER` as the range's card is, with the doll (PERSONNEL's and the inventory's drawing) at 96 px a metre and faint man-target zones, each impact marked and numbered, the newest ringed red, from one helper `hitMarks` that also draws H's doll and PERSONNEL's; what the shooter could tell during the fight, the truth after "Search him"; three samples to score.
  - **Where it is built** (ROADMAP §4.2, §5.8): a new row **0.0.7c** after `test-pairs`, in two patches: `test-pairs&setup=bangs` "Three bangs" (the anchor, scored alone first, `combat-race` with it), then `test-stress` "Bang" (five strings with the coach's noise, ear defenders, Breathe, the hit card on turners); then **5c `test-breach`** "Bang, then go" after `test-shoothouse` (the defenders on role-players, the poses, the hit card on people). About 700, 1,400 and 1,600 new lines.
  - **Answered and restated**: COMBAT §24 question 14 answered with its default (calm at 10 m, only speed changes); the user's "If so yes" on targets turning to face you is NPCs' (§7.11); question 15 (a turn in the lull on `test-pairs`) restated plainly as a yes or no, its default still no there and yes on `test-stress`'s string 5; VIEWS §18 question 20's remainder restated plainly.
  - **Open for the user**: COMBAT §24 questions 16 to 23 (the name; the test sheets' values; noise never pinning the hardened; friendly fire; the startle's keys; what a bang buys and who wears filtered visors; two bangs breaking an insurgent; the anchor scene without ear defenders) and VIEWS §18 questions 21 and 22 (the hit card's look; belief, then truth), each with its default.

Designed in full the same day in COMBAT §25 (the close-quarters round: three proposals, two judges, one synthesis), each rule (my call) unless it quotes the user, for the user to overrule (COMBAT §3, §7.7, §7.8, §25; SKILLS §6, §11.1, §14, §15.1; WEAPONS §5.3, §7; TIME §4.3; VIEWS §7.4, §10; CAREER §4.6, §4.7, §5.4, §5.8, §9.3; PLACES §1.6, §4.12, §13; ROADMAP §4.2, §5.8; BODY §16.1; PEOPLE §7.6; CONTRACTS §9, §11, §13):
  - **Skill shows as automation, and training and experience are two things** (the user's two words). Training is a drill family's **rung** (room, passage, hatch, void; 0 untrained, 1 taught, 2 practised, 3 drilled, 4 fluent): taught by a teacher who teaches only so far (unit training to 2; the shoot house's cadre, the boarding course and the AIC to 3; the Raiders' ITC to 4), practised in clean runs (20 for rung 2, 60 for 3; a 3 h block is about 12, lived or skipped the same), and **each bundle lived once by hand before it is offered** (the user's "once we did it once, remember it", tradecraft, 2026-10-26). Experience is Warfighting, which sets the take-in. The new join is rung 1, taught by the School of Infantry's MOUT on the summary paper.
  - **T's reach grows with the rung; the steps stay single.** Rungs 0 and 1: one step a press (Stack on the door; Open), and **never the walk in**, which is X and the move keys (the user's recruit); rung 2 a group (Stack and open; Enter and clear your corner; Dominate); 3 the room; 4 the house. A bundle is a named drill whose beats are shown, ticked and rolled, halting with "Carry on"; it **never fires, never spends what its row does not name, never picks a target, never reloads unasked**, and stops at every person seen, an unplanned door, the gun, a failed roll that matters, an order or a hit. The method is the player's (cross or hook, open or kick, slice, limited penetration, flash first). From rung 3 the line has no speaker: the drill instructor's voice becomes the marine's own thought.
  - **Contact pauses the game after the marine's own take-in, one helper for the player and every NPC** (`takeInMs`, COMBAT §25.4, replacing COMBAT §3.2's reaction delay and §3.3's noticing late and startle): Warfighting's core `30 + 700 × (1 − WF / 100)²` ms times the expectation (his sector ×1, a room he is clearing ×1.5, alert ×2, surprised ×3), an Awareness check's grade, who it might be (×1.5 with the hands unseen or a no-shoot beside him), how many, stance, light and the body, plus 200 ms a suppression band, in whole ticks, never under 0.15 s alert or 0.25 s surprised. **The user's numbers**: a private in a room he is clearing 0.80 s (1.20 s on a natural 1), a boot recruit 0.95 to 1.45 s, a tier one 0.05 s; a five-year Raider 0.10 s in his sector. The pause is the decision becoming available; the round still leaves at a human time (the tier one's 0.30 s after the sighting).
  - **During the take-in** the world runs at 1:1 (the user: "the world runs on meanwhile"), every key but Space and Esc is refused, hollow amber brackets close round the person, an untrained marine's feet brake after his body's go and a pre-armed one's carry on. **The pause always comes** and never says who is hostile: in a clear every person newly seen pauses, an identification roll says only what the marine could tell ("a rifle", "hands empty", "can't see his hands"), and Challenge and Hold him stand beside the shots. A kept reflex answer never answers it with a shot.

Reloads by purpose: RELOAD on empty, Top up in a lull, RELOAD keep the empty; "eyes up" from Weapon handling 15; "Gun down!"; the transition with the sidearm (0.5).
  - **Stances in the room**: Combat 0.97 m/s on Mars, the walking shot −4, −2 from Marksmanship 15; Assault 1.60 m/s, close-quarters fire within 10 m from rung 2 at `mitigated(−8, Marksmanship, 30, 0.25)`, else suppression only (the user's "close quarters shooting for experienced operators").   - **A scene never dead-ends**: a shooter with no rounds left sits the string out (the coach checks and says so; the string scores as not fired) and the course goes on to its end and its score card.

Fire it."), and a string fired with more than its rounds is remarked on, never praised as normal (my call).
  - **A magazine is reached for before it goes in**: "Reach for a magazine" from a chosen pouch brings it to the free hand; in hand you learn what it holds (about how many by its weight, the count and the kind of rounds by a look at its lips and witness holes), then you insert it, put it away again, dump it or drop it. The drills (LOAD, RELOAD) are built from those steps and pick the magazine by rule, and the steps stay available one by one.
