The 25 m line is a six-lane bay of its own in X3's north strip beside the tower, firing beside the KD lanes, never across them, so nothing moves at 0.0.7 (item 2); it lies on `melas.ground`, grown over X3 and walked in two rects, the gates shut, the scene starting on the line (item 3); the range pieces are the ground's `pieces`, not catalogue fixtures, `walkM` stays Main Side's with `walksM` the list, and the session names its firing point `fp`, a fixture's name, not a `spotId` (PLACES §4.12, TECH §6.1). The cards are drawn three times life size; a red flag on a pole at each end of the line, since the tower's is out of the weapon mode's frame; the tower's deck open under a half shade; the berm stops walkers, not eyes; **the coach stands at one spot for every stance, (fp.x − 0.9, fp.z − 1.4) facing downrange**, at your left shoulder and drawn behind you (the stage's and the fixer's, after the build sheet's spot and the stage's both covered you). The line is yours alone, a new join's familiarisation fire, the relay waiting for `gunner.js` (item 4); three magazines of five, prone, kneeling, standing, on the card at `targetDC` +6, DC 19, scored by WEAPONS §11.1's rings (item 5); **the set stoppage is the coach's and procedural**, a dud, a stovepipe or a double on the 2nd or 3rd magazine's 2nd to 4th round from `stream(seed, 'arms:set', course)` (item 6). The range officer and the coach are generated on `stream('staff', seed, site, post)`, tier 3, their sheets by their own rank's tier, so a corporal coach can read below the sergeant's Awareness (items 23, 24; PEOPLE §3.13, SKILLS §11.1). The scene's end leaves stop motion and slings the rifle (item 25); no v0.0.4 save needs an upgrade, and two are added to `tests/saves/` as proof (item 30).
  - **Stop motion and the clock.** The gun's own stop in a lived step offers the reflex card, its record `state.stop.reflex`, saved (item 8; COMBAT §3.5, TIME §13); G with no weapon keeps v0.0.4's stub, `ready` and `digit` moving to the weapons domain, so the rifle's work changes nothing in a gunless game, made byte for byte as v0.0.4's (item 9; played, it differs from v0.0.4's only by the patch round's eyes and rack, the 2026-11-06 entries below). Sub-step boundaries are taken in the fast `input` layer, not `rules`, so the body counts no tick the clock did not pass (the shot role's); a step lives `ceil(Σ / 50)` ticks, UNLOAD 5,050 ms. **The chained wait**: a step completing the tower's command runs on to the next (15 s at most), any step or X cutting it (item 16; the integrator's for X). The tower's commands are O events at priority 2, ending a wait or a hold at once, CEASE FIRE any step at its sub-step's end (item 17; `onRangeCmd(state, bus, ev)`). The in-between stances: stand to kneel and back 500 ms, prone to kneel 1,000, prone to stand 1,300 (COMBAT §3.4). Tempo on the digits: tapped normal, Shift rushed, held careful, Fire held hold and fire, Hold a step of its own (item 15). **R offers nothing while stop motion is on or a range session is open** (the integrator's and the fixer's; TIME §5.3).
  - **The rifle.** Thumb work takes no `LONG_K`, and the macros' times are one formula's, 2.41, 3.10, 1.57 and 7.90 s for WEAPONS §5.3's 2.3, 2.7, 1.3 and 8 (item 13; SHOW CLEAR 7.89 of formula, 7.90 lived). **Diagnosis is a step**: `observe`, 0.3 s at DC 4, unpaced, ends IMMEDIATE and REMEDIAL ACTION and stands alone; SHOW CLEAR puts the selector on semi before its function check (item 14). **REMEDIAL ACTION is its steps**, lock back, strip the magazine into the hand (0.85 s, DC 6), rack ×3 (0.45 s, DC 5), the magazine in, charge, observe, 6.05 s at Weapon handling 10 (the design review's); lock back keeps a double believed. **With the hammer believed down, a macro's first safe comes after its first cocking step, or a charge goes before it, so CEASE FIRE charges, chambering a round from a magazine** (the fixer's; WEAPONS open question 10). A look that finds a round makes "Carry on" lock back and look again first. SHOW CLEAR is offered only once the tower says it, the ◉ marks only the drill that completes the command, and the coach speaks at his look's end (the integrator's). `conditionOf` ignores the port cover; a magazine's believed count lives on the gun while it is in and on the magazine when out; `REFUSALS` gains `open` and `dead`; a missed lock back leaves a live round in the chamber; the bad inspection is logged hidden and halts nothing; `doAction` applies `fire`'s −3 and not yet `danger`'s −1 (the arms role's). The selector leads the singles in the panel; Charge is offered by tradecraft's rule; a shot that does not cycle keeps its case until a step clears it (the integrator's and the fixer's). WEAPONS §5.4's table gives way to the `nd` test's grid, which routine work and the silent inspection make unlike the first (WEAPONS open question 11).
  - **Carry and safety.** Range safety is tradecraft's rules with three of the Corps' (ahead of the tower, a cold line, the finger while moving), Rule 1 holding loaded or not (item 18); range safety never checks a slung rifle, while the habit runs for slung rifles too (the arms role's); an ND follows the posture's line, graded ND-1 to ND-3, a line through a person ND-3 and passing them (item 19), a function check's along the aimed, level, unspread line into the berm, uprange ND-3. **On the line with the rifle in hand the walking keys strafe without turning**, and a move key in stop motion is no move for the finger rule (the fixer's). The muzzle tips by posture are the arms role's, the stage drawing the flash and the case at its own drawn muzzle.
  - **The shot.** Every roll on the range is at pressure `evaluated` (item 12); skills never import the body, `rollerOf` bringing the body's and the needs' modifiers (item 11). The shot's stream is keyed by the shot's id too, since a misfire counted no round and repeated forever (the shot role's fix; WEAPONS §7.11). **On a paper target the plaque's figure is the chance of a hole**, the roll's pass kept as `rollOdds` (the fixer's, after the parity review; WEAPONS open question 9). Holes are hashed with the world's seed. The case flies horizontally at 4 m/s, 60° off the bore to the right (item 20). The coach's string lines count the shots fired, his tip follows how the string was shot, and the DI's "front sight" is the reticle (the integrator's and the fixer's).
  - **The instinct.** It speaks every tone under the `'stop'` hold, urgent only under the others, its lead "Time stands still." (item 10); the range's rules carry their own `repeatMs` 0, the line settles when stop motion's phase or step changes, and `range-stoppage` has a `look` variant (the shot role's); the DI reads the heading from the facing outside the mode, the base rules stay quiet below urgent for the whole session, ended included, and **T aims and goes off safe but never takes the shot** (the integrator's and the fixer's).
  - **The view and the HUD.** The weapon mode's lead goes 0.55 of the way to the bracketed target, keeping 2.5 m from the edge, and a target is in frame when bracketing it would show it (item 26); **your shot shakes the picture 2 px**, not VIEWS's 1 (the fixer's); `FLASH_MS` 100, a 9 × 5 star at the drawn muzzle, the blast's dust off the regolith, cases two pixels with a first-tick glint; the rifle bands in an atlas pool of their own (8 sheets at 16 px/m, 9 at 24), the range staff's dress a duty dress since the 24 px/m lineup is full. PAPER sits under PAUSED's slot, RADIO on STEPS, the reflex card beside the weapon card, the running step shown alone, the HUD reading the sim's views once a tick; the mouse finds targets through the stage's camera; the ear defenders are electronic, impulses only; the radio box's two lines are two speakers'; the weapon card at a whole number of device pixels a card pixel, its caption only where it cannot paint; by the keys the plaque names Fire's digit and shows its list for 2.5 s, and hides on a cold line or under a handling step; J holds 400 lines, newest first, each roll its words first, hidden rolls left out; I's reach is the sim's Magazine in time; **a new card takes no Enter or Space for its first 400 ms** (the hud role's, the integrator's and the fixer's).
  - **Left as built** (for a later change): the hole, the case and the flash at the fire step's start, not 0.15 s into it; on a double feed the belief's chamber stays `live` under DOUBLE; the figures atlas at its 15 looks at 24 px/m; the I card's doll without the carrier, the ear defenders or the rifle; the quick suite's 60 s deep in the background band. The stair wells' fix and the test-night requests ship in v0.0.5 too, built apart in a patch round and merged before the release (the 2026-11-06 entries below).
  - **Open for the user** (WEAPONS's open questions 9 to 11): the plaque's figure over a card (the chance of a hole, or the DC's pass with the edge ring scored from the DC); the R-7's lever that will not reach safe with the hammer down, so CEASE FIRE charges first; and whether routine handling on the range should keep a natural 1's teeth, since as built no rested private or sergeant ever fires a negligent discharge there. The scores of "Show clear", the Windows bench (`?bench=range-line`) and the sound are the user's to give (docs/PLAYTESTS.md). From the patch round: the klaxon through dark-adapted eyes, 1.34 times as light as the picture the user scored (VIEWS §18, question 12; the 2026-11-06 entry on `test-night` below).

- 2026-11-06, the user on the game's name, inspired by Olympus Mons: "We will figure out the role for Olympus Mons organically, as long as it appears on maps in game it's already fine". The rule (SETTING §4.1, VIEWS §6.5): **every map of Mars the game shows draws Olympus Mons and names it** (the planetary display, briefing and route maps, the wall maps and posters in the handcrafted places). Its part in play is left open, to be found as the game grows.
- 2026-11-06, the user on the stairs, playing v0.0.4, with a shot of the upper floor's east stair well: "florr N-1, cannot cross the space to head downstairs again, is it intentional ?", then "stairs at level N (starting level) are fine, it's when I try going down to N-2 from N-1 that I'm stuck". Not intentional: two faults. (1) **The upper floor's wells were drawn back to front**: their lit landing and arrow at the hall door's end, while the walkable top and the way down are at the far end, where one arrives from below (the art maker and the sim's blocker read the piece's facing two opposite ways). (2) **Up and down did not read**: a flight's arrow showed the walk direction, so the ground floor's flights, climbed walking south, pointed down the screen, and nothing named the floor, so the user took the upper floor for a lower one. The rules (PLACES, VIEWS): **a stair well is drawn open exactly where the sim lets one step in**, from one convention the art and the sim share and a test holds; **flights carry "▲ UP" and wells "▼ DOWN"**, whatever way they run, readable at 16 px/m; **the HUD's place line names the floor** ("Echo Company barracks, upper floor"). **Fixed in v0.0.5**, by a patch round built apart from the range with the test-night requests below and merged before the release (PLACES §1.5, §4.4, §9.6; VIEWS §2.9, §7.1). Claude's calls in it, each (my call): **the picture was fixed to the sim, not the sim to the picture** (you arrive at the flight's top, the south, and walk north down it; nothing in the sim moved), the kit drawing the landing, its mark, the dark well and the guard from the rule the sim's blocker keeps (`stairEnd`), and `places-stairs` holding the two equal in every area; **the marks are upright and amber**, "▲ UP" at a flight's foot and "▼ DOWN" on a well's landing, a solid triangle over the word on a dark plate; **the upper floor is called "upper floor"**, not "first floor" (a name that says where it is from the ground); **the watch shows the place on two lines**, the building and floor over the room, so the line never wraps at its dot; and the places validator checks that every way between areas lands you facing the way you walk (`arrive-face`) and that a well's way down fires only on its landing (`stair-top`). The user's other asks of the same day (the single-action interactions with R's quick action and the multi-select menu, and the hair) are built in v0.0.6, on top of v0.0.5's range, whose rifle work changes R and the keys too (my call: building them alongside it would make two versions of the same keys to reconcile).
- 2026-11-06, the user on hair, with a shot of a balding marine (v0.0.4's `bald` cut, a horseshoe of hair round a bare crown): "this haircut is not military acceptable, we should have to groom our hair the same way we will have to groom our beards and a soldier with half balding hair would 100% go fully shaved head for service." The rules (CAREER §2's appearance, BODY §6, PEOPLE §11.10, VIEWS §3), a patch request, built in v0.0.6 (the stairs entry above; v0.0.4's `bald` cut is still in v0.0.5):
  - **Only regulation cuts**, as CAREER §2 already lists them and v0.0.4's looks did not: men high and tight, low regulation, medium regulation, or shaved; women a regulation bun, short, or a braid pinned up. **A man whose hair is thinning shaves his head**: the horseshoe `bald` cut is gone, and the roster's receding hairlines (by age) draw the shaved head.
  - **Hair grows and is groomed like the beard**: a length on the body that grows every second, lived or skipped, shown on the figure and the doll and in words, read by the instinct ("Haircut's due Thursday.") and by the inspections (PEOPLE §11.10: a haircut within 10 sols). A shaved head is kept at the basin with clippers, a single action like the face's shave; the other cuts at the PX's barber (PLACES §4.13's three chairs). A new join arrives fresh from the School of Infantry's barber.
  - The squad keeps its own hair in regulation by PEOPLE's closed-form barber need, out of sight.
  - **Built in v0.0.6** (BODY §2.5, §6; CAREER §4.2; PEOPLE §5.5, §6.2, §11.10; VIEWS §3.4, §3.9, §4.2, §8.2; PLACES §4.13; SKILLS §14.4): the seven regulation cuts, men and women sharing none; thinning drawn by hash against `THIN_P` by age and drawn as the shaved head, the horseshoe gone (v0.0.5 saves' `bald` men mapped to `shaved`); the hair's hours since the cut grown every second, read through `hairOf` and drawn at four steps on the figure, the sleeper and the doll; the DI's due and over lines by day; the clippers at a sink for a shaved head and, for the other cuts, **a barber's pole standing by the PX's north door** (the PX has no inside yet, so its three chairs wait for the building); the squad's barber in closed form, a cut never seen happening. Regulation is now **men within 10 sols, women's short within 21, a bun or a braid within 42** (my call, amending the 10 sols for everyone above). The cuts' look waits for the user's scores (`samples/cuts/`, VIEWS §18, question 16).
- 2026-11-06, the user on moving with a weapon: "I'm okay with the current walk speed as a "we're relaxed and in no rush" speed. But there will need to be stances that include "Relaxed", "Combat", "Assault" which will influence how fast we walk and how fast we run, with combat walking being the default way we move with weapons up like irl, allowing to acquire and engage while moving to cover. Assault walking would be significantly faster and would not allow accurate shooting but would allow suppressing as we move to cover, or close quarters shooting for experienced operators." The rules (BODY §16.1, WEAPONS §6.1 and §7.3, COMBAT §5, VIEWS §10):
  - **Three movement stances, each with its own walk and run**: **Relaxed**, v0.0.4's gaits as they are (on Mars a 1.14 m/s walk, 2.69 m/s run, 0.75 m/s stroll), the weapon carried safe; **Combat**, the default whenever you move with a weapon up, as in real life, able to acquire and engage on the move to cover; **Assault**, significantly faster, with no accurate fire but suppressive fire on the move to cover, and close-quarters shooting for experienced operators (the moving-fire penalty eased by skill and training).
  - They are not the body's height (stand, crouch, kneel, prone: the A and W keys), which combines with them. The stance sets the carry posture it moves in (Relaxed slung or patrol, Combat low ready, Assault shouldered; WEAPONS §6.1), and the shot's moving-shooter modifier by stance (WEAPONS §7.3); assault fire counts as suppression (COMBAT §5) (my call on the mapping).
  - My calls on the numbers and keys, to be measured when built: Combat's walk a little under Relaxed's (the heel-to-toe glide with the rifle up), its run the rush between covers; Assault's walk well above Relaxed's, which on Mars passes the walk-to-lope line at 1.33 m/s (BODY §16.1's Froude 0.5), so the assault pace at 0.38 G is a low lope. V cycles the three stances, the fine carry postures staying on V held (VIEWS §10.1).
  - When (my call): Relaxed is what exists. Combat and Assault are built with the first scene where you move and shoot (COMBAT's training on Base Melas in 0.1.x), and nothing built before then may assume one walk speed for a person carrying a weapon.
- 2026-11-06, the user on interactions, after playing v0.0.4: "the shave+wash+water when pressing R at a basin without the tootip telling us what is going to happen is no good. First of all, implement ways of combining actions and quick action needs to be the most obvious. For the basin, first of all using the head shouldn't wash our hands either afterwards, then going to the basin, R should wash hands if hygiene is low enough, drink if we're thristy and shave if none of the above (use a priority thing and R is the most urgent), long pressing R should open the menu with all the possible interractions as single actions, with the ability to select several actions (not pre-combined actions like you do now that make lists hard to read), implement pixel art menu drop downs or checkboxes for multiselections". The rules, for every fixture and object (VIEWS §10, TIME §8), a patch request, built in v0.0.6 (the stairs entry above):
  - **R is the quick action: one single action, the most urgent there, chosen by a priority**, and the offer line always says which before you press ("R wash hands · 30 s"). Nothing pressed with R does more than it says.
  - **Actions are single, never pre-combined.** The basin's bundle of teeth, a shave and the face (v0.0.4's `basin`) is split into its actions, and no list shows a bundle.
  - **R held opens the menu of every single action at hand, with several selectable at once**, run one after another behind one splash, with the total time and the end shown before you confirm.
  - **Menus are pixel art**: the game's own drop-downs and checkboxes, drawn in its look, never the browser's controls.
  - **The head does not wash your hands**; the basin does. At a basin R's priority is: wash your hands if they need it, drink if you are thirsty, shave if neither (where stubble shows).
  - **Built in v0.0.6** (TIME §6.4, §8.1, §8.2, §8.4, §9.5; VIEWS §7.1, §8.10, §10.1, §11.3; SKILLS §14.1, §14.4; BODY §6; the build sheet `docs/build/0.0.6.md`): R's plate on every instinct line, urgent ones included, saying what R does and how long before it is pressed; `PRIORITY` per spot as data, at a sink the user's order (dirty hands, thirst, stubble) then a shaved head that is due, and nothing due the wait; the basin's bundle split into six single actions and sleep's turn-in into dressing, both kept `legacy`; the head soils the hands, a sink washes them (the hands' own ledger); R held opens a pixel-art menu with checkboxes and drop-downs, the total and the end from TIME's plan, and several rows run as one chain behind one splash, Backspace stopping it after the running step.
- 2026-11-06, the user's score of `test-night` (v0.0.4, seed 2513373485): night 80, klaxon 80, drill 60, not starred (docs/PLAYTESTS.md, v0.0.4). Their words and what follows from them:
  - **"Needs minor refining as the whole scene looks rushed, slightly slower animations on getting in and out of rack"**: a patch request, built in v0.0.5 (a patch round with the stairs' fix: the entry above; PEOPLE §4.6, TIME §8). **The rack's lived transitions are 1.6 times as long at the alarm's tempo**, the player's and the squad's alike: 4.0 s from a lower berth, 5.5 s from an upper, lying down the same (v0.0.4's `RISE_MS` 2.5 s, 3.4 from an upper berth), and `UNHURRIED_K` 1.2 times more when nothing hurries (the weekend's reveille, the stand-down, going to bed, up after a nap). The drill's whole sequence was looked at for what else reads as rushed, its timeline otherwise kept (the count at 02:35, the stand-down at 03:00). Claude's calls in it, each (my call): `UNHURRIED_K`; **a lower berth's 1.55 m to its foot is walked, not slid** (v0.0.4 slid it at 3.1 m/s); **the page's R lies you down in sight before a sleep or a nap, and a move key takes it back** (the same note's "getting in": v0.0.4 put you in the berth on the splash's first frame); **the klaxon's call delays are 1 to 10 s, drawn per call from the seed and its moment** (after the drill's 60: in v0.0.4 a quick T, T caught 2 of the 12 rising, and the same marine rose first every night of every career).
  - **"night and klaxon lose points simply because I don't understand what is going on right away, which would be fine if we have (only the first time in tutorial which will take place in basic training) an explaination of what we're doing"**: the first time a system meets the player it is explained, once, in a tutorial that takes place in basic training. CAREER §4.6's playable flashback at MCRD Argyre is where that lives; how far it grows (whether it takes over the teaching CAREER §5 gives the standby's squad leader) is an open question to the user, below. Test scenes explain nothing more than their goal line.
  - **"Otherwise night and klaxon are pretty perfect."** The night's light, the slip, the klaxon's landing and its beat stand as built.
  - **"Would be nice to have eye adapting to darkness making things less dark after a while in the dark compared to when entering dark from a lit place (additional idea global to a game where we will have combat helmets and suits with night vision built in so it only makes sense when not wearing those."**: the eyes' dark adaptation, global to the game, a patch request. Each person's eyes carry an adaptation state that follows the light they see, slowly into the dark (the cones in minutes, the rods over half an hour) and quickly back into light; perception's reach by light and the picture's exposure both read it, so the fog and the picture agree (one quantity, one number). A red lamp, such as the fire watch's, spares the rods (my call: the reason navies and armies use red light at night). With a helmet's or a suit's night vision on, the picture is the device's and the eye's adaptation is not shown; it keeps running underneath, so taking the device off shows the eye as it is. **Built in v0.0.5**, in the patch round with the stairs' fix (the entry above; BODY §23.4, PEOPLE §7.1, VIEWS §2.7): dark adaptation for every body, the picture and the reach reading one number. Claude's calls in it, each (my call): luminance goes as `lightLevel` cubed (Stevens' 0.33), 0.75 being 100 cd/m²; **`REF_LOG` 0.15 makes v0.0.4's picture the eye five minutes in from the lit head**, so the klaxon wakes you fully dark-adapted at 2.4 to 2.8 times v0.0.4's gain (the rods') and 1.07 (the cones'), and the bay in sight is 1.34 times as light as the picture the user scored 80; a glare blooms the picture but not perception; no upgrade writes the eyes on load; the rods' grey goes at most half way.
  - Claude's calls in the review of the eyes and the rack, by the fixer, each (my call): **the picture takes two gains**, the rods' for what the rods see of a pixel and the cones' for the rest, mixed by the pixel's dimness and its scotopic over photopic luminance (the fire watch's red pool 1.27 times v0.0.4's brightness, 1.61 with one gain); a splash holds the eye as it began until its zoom out; the player's eye through a transition is the pose shown; your own berth (within `ALWAYS_M` of the body, by a standing eye's clear line) is always in your sight while you look out of it; the view forgets its memory for a skip only under a full splash's closed iris; the sitter on the mattress slides to the berth's edge over the last 60% of sitting up. **Known and open**: the red pool's hue still leans toward orange at the higher brightness (19 degrees against v0.0.4's 11), and the full fix is the light maps carrying each light's S/P per pixel.
  - Answered 2026-11-07 by the user's score of v0.0.5's `test-night` (klaxon 100, "much better"): keep the dark-adapted wake. Was the open question (VIEWS §18, question 12): keep the dark-adapted wake, or cap the rods' gain so the klaxon shows as scored? The two side by side are ready as a numbered sample (`/private/tmp/claude-501/olympus-build/fixer-er/ab-klaxon.png`); a cap would be the picture's rod gain (for example 1.5), never perception's `lightK`. Default until they say: keep, their own ask.
  - Open question to the user: does the basic-training tutorial take over the standby's teaching (the squad leader's teaching beats, CAREER §5), or does it explain only the first things a recruit meets (a night, the klaxon, the shower, the rifle) while the standby keeps its squad leader? Default until they say: the second, the flashback grown into a short first-time tutorial at Argyre (my call).

The klaxon is a horn in hall-2 at (57.7, 8.8) on floor 1 with a red beacon, the bay left dark (item 23). The night's events need a roster, so the sandbox posts none (item 26); the `local@20:00` job posts the night.
  - **The body.** One door, `bodySync`, before every change, live minutes on the tick-averaged load saved in `body.held` (item 8); rates held per minute cell, rounding on the grid only. The body clock is mean solar time at 24 body hours a sol plus `offsetH`, smooth through the slip (item 9). The start body is the template walked along `REFERENCE_SOL` to the start's hour (item 10), and the template is that sol's own steady state at a training reveille (bladder 160 mL, transit 100 g, grime 32, balance −0.2 L, `emptyH` 2.0). A meal's water is released as the gut absorbs the food (without it every meal sent the bladder to urgent 1.5 h later). The reference sol eats 900, 900 and 1,000 kcal (a holiday's brunch 1,200 at 08:00 and dinner 1,100) and goes to a head about every two hours, voiding from `HEAD_VOID_FROM_ML` 300 at a head and 200 at a stall. The latency's doubling counts only the minutes inside the wake-maintenance zone, and `depthK` is fixed at the onset. Gaits are rounded to 0.01 m/s, so Mars stays 1.14, 2.69 and 0.75 (item 11). An outfit's warmth is tradecraft's 3.3 × clo until the two-node model (0.0.12). `bodyMods` holds thermal and stamina, `needMods` the needs, kept apart by `src` (BODY §22). Urinating is TIME §8.2's flat 75 s, and `bodyMods`' perception term goes unread (item 21). Stubble fails an inspection from 20 h inclusive, and a woman's body grows no stubble (`hasBeard`; item 37, after the review: a woman marine was told to shave).
  - **Activities and the splash.** T acts only where its act is at hand (item 12). No game time passes from the landing to the end of the zoom out, under a `'splash'` pause never shown as PAUSED, and a fast-forward's brief splash runs at the engine's pace (item 13); the ease's ends run at 40% of the middle's rate (item 14). R's wait is TIME §7.3's stand-by in 15-minute chunks (item 16). Dressing takes `0.6 × DRESS_MIN[from] + DRESS_MIN[to]`, and sleep turns in to PT kit first (item 17). The morning routine is withdrawn this round (item 35); no sleep is offered past reveille or where it would not end on it, and the head only above `URINATE_MIN_ML` 50 mL (item 36). A resumed shower puts back what was worn into it, and a part shortened by a fit or a resume keeps its whole length, its effects going by the share lived (item 27); nothing starts while the player's rise plays (item 28); a night ended by its own reveille shows its strip (item 34). The debug skips take TIME's short 1.0 s lapse, an interrupted splash shows no strip, and the bench card times frames by rAF's timestamps (item 42). Test scenes save nothing (item 24); their end card waits 6 s of play or Enter (item 38), a cut activity does not end a scene, the scores keep the last 200 and an empty note reads "note: none".

Inside a closed iris the fog lifts, the shower stall and its spot are cut to 1 m and the see-through lets go, the bather staying in the stall through the dress part (item 39); a toilet stall's boots and OCCUPIED slide are drawn at its door; effect clocks run on real time. A shaved head shows a wet glint, and the sheet says "Head wet." (the integrator's).

While a drill is open only the alarm, the urgent needs and the stand-fast line speak (the integrator's: mustered, the DI had said "Shave. Head A has mirrors."). Past the count the alarm says you missed it; no grooming lines from 21:50 to 05:00; woken in the night, back to sleep or the head; tired before taps is a note; no squad board by day; "T held" only on a chip with an act; T with no line seen does nothing; no humour in place of the drill's line (item 41).

(a) count the zone on the civil clock, (b) move taps to 21:45, or (c) accept the model and keep BODY §7.1's corrected text. Default until answered: (c), with `body-sleep` holding 0.4 to 2.0 h.

- 2026-11-06, Claude's calls in carrying the user's two rules through the sections:
  - **How places are authored.** Layout, dressing and ground tools only propose a place while it is being authored. What they make is committed as fixed data and finished by hand. Fine variation within a place comes from a hash of its coordinates or ids, never from the world's seed. Every places golden is taken under two world seeds and must match, and no site, area or ground record carries a seed (v0.0.2's per-site seed goes; PLACES §9, TECH §13.5).
  - **What is authored and what is drawn.** The history up to the career's start is authored and shared by every world: SETTING §7.7's archive, the Europa crisis as it stands at the start, and the past's figures named as history. From the start on, everything that happens is procedural, and the headlines name offices whose holders the world generates. The Commandant and every other public office holder are generated per world, and General Halima Strand is withdrawn (SETTING §1.1, §7.7; CONTRACTS §17). A unit's billets and a site's posts are authored; who fills them is drawn, and the staff are drawn by `stream('staff', worldSeed, site, post)` (PEOPLE §3.13). Legends are authored histories whose person is generated when chosen (CAREER §22).
  - **The first career's opening.** A first career's first deployment is the Europa stabilisation tour, because the brief's opening and the milestones are built in that order. When its warning order comes inside the standby's "week or two" (10 + slip sols, CAREER §5.4), the orders' details and everything after them are drawn. The user may overrule this.
  - **Missions in authored places.** Operations place objectives, barricades, hostages and danger areas on spots tagged in the authored hulls, settings and patrol routes, so no layout is rolled (COMBAT §23, PLACES §9). The shoot house's panel layout for an exercise is a mission detail.
  - **Smaller calls.** The people atlas uses VIEWS §3.7's R8 role-index layers (48 MiB at 24 px/m, less at the chosen 16). BODY's G-qualification follows CAREER §7.3's gates. The skip record carries `skip.engine`, and an `out` skip in a fight is a fast-forward. A building's portal takes the site plan's door id. `PATH_NODES_TICK` may come down toward 1,800 if `mess-noon` measures the paths layer over 0.60 ms. `state.mind` is read and written through PEOPLE's accessors (PEOPLE §8.8). Field day runs daily aboard ship in the `maintenance` block (PEOPLE §4.7). The base's roads adopt PLACES's working names (SETTING §4.9). ROADMAP's moment 1 is "Reporting as ordered". Generated households are drawn into authored homes by `stream('household', worldSeed, site, home)` (PEOPLE §12.4). A large authored place tags at least three operation spots of each kind (objective, barricade, hostage, insertion, extraction; PLACES §9.8).

### Carried-over tastes (the user's, from LONGHAUL and tradecraft)

Sections cite these here. They hold in OLYMPUS until the user says otherwise.

- **No fast travel that skips play** (tradecraft rc50, rc51; 2026-10-29: "we should not have the option to just fast travel to work, it would skip all the gameplay").
- **No abstraction where an action could be lived**, and "time should feel like a currency" (tradecraft, 2026-10-27).
- **Nothing consumed or spent unasked** (tradecraft, 2026-10-31).
- **We only know what we know** (tradecraft, 2026-10-26): screens, maps and the HUD show only what the character could know.
- **Nothing pops in or out of view** (the user's report from Windows, tradecraft, 2026-10-26).
- **A splash and a reflex chance before a big outcome lands** (tradecraft, 2026-11-04).
- **Graphics over words, and no overlapping UI** (tradecraft, 2026-11-04).
- **Keys by physical position, labelled AZERTY** (tradecraft, 2026-10-26: "don't give me the QWERTY keybinds").
- **Every roll readable in the log; deep, stateful gear** (tradecraft, 2026-11-03).
- **NPCs with real destinations** (tradecraft, 2026-10-26).
- **At least three kinds of everything** (undated), and "insane variety in mission descriptions" (tradecraft rc52).
- **Pixel art 100%, but simple, almost 2D** (tradecraft, 2026-10-25), and no generated images (2026-11-05, above).
